# Idea 07 — Recount
> Seed: 7. [mild] **Farming × mine tailings × carbon credits.** Enhanced rock weathering MRV for small farmers.

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Recount

**Hook:** Measure carbon the way Colorado audits elections. Pool hundreds of small farms, pick fields to core by public lottery, and stop sampling once the pool's claimed tonnage is statistically confirmed.

**The isomorphism.** ERW's bottleneck is MRV. Soil carbonate and cation losses vary so much across a field that verifying a 20-hectare farm can cost more than its credits earn. Election officials faced the same shape. Hand-counting every ballot is too costly, and trusting the machine count is too risky. Philip Stark's **risk-limiting audits** sample paper ballots one by one until the reported result is confirmed at, say, a 5% risk limit. So a pool's claimed tonnes are the reported margin and a soil core is a paper ballot. Audit cost then scales with *discrepancy*, not with the number of farms.

**The loop:**
1. **Match.** Each farm is paired with the nearest assayed feedstock, either mine tailings or quarry fines, within a haul radius where trucking emissions don't eat the credit. Every load carries an XRF passport covering mineralogy and a nickel and chromium screen.
2. **Spread and declare.** The farmer logs truck tickets, spreader GPS or phone photos, and the application rate. That's the "machine count".
3. **Lottery.** A public random draw picks fields and grid cells to core. A sequential test runs until the pool's claim holds or fails.
4. **Pay.** Credits are issued at the confidence lower bound and split pro rata. Fields that miss trigger targeted re-audits and clawbacks.

**Why now.** Frontier approved Puro.earth's ERW methodology on June 22, 2026. Climeworks and Lithos signed an offtake in March 2026, and credits trade around €200–500/t (brief §14, price soft). The brief names small-farm ERW MRV as an unbuilt gap (§14, §16 #7). Open problem for the Builder: unlike ballots, soil cores are read *with noise*.

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** Smallholder ERW pooling already won: **Mati Carbon** took the $50M XPRIZE Carbon Removal grand prize (2025) spreading basalt on Indian rice farms. **Terradot**, **InPlanet**, **UNDO**, **Eion** and **Lithos** all run farmer networks with in-house MRV. **Isometric** and Puro protocols decide which sampling counts. The cautionary tale is soil organic carbon: Nori shut down in 2024 amid sampling disputes. Nobody sells *the audit design itself*, and that's the opening.

**Hardest problem: a core reads biased, late and partial, not just noisy.** An RLA assumes the audited ballot reveals the truth. A soil core gives cation loss with a lab error bar on top of spatial heterogeneity that dwarfs the signal at 10–40 t/ha. It's read years before weathering finishes and can't see river and ocean losses. The "reported margin" is itself a model output, so a sequential test on raw cores either never stops or certifies a bias.

**Tailings reality check.** Ultramafic tailings carry nickel and chromium and often need waste-exemption permits. Basalt fines are the mainstream feedstock. Make tailings a second lane, not the hook.

**MVP.** Recount is not a registry. It's the sampling-design layer for one existing developer under the Isometric or Puro protocol, with one co-op of about 60 farms sharing one quarry. The draw is a public dice ceremony, as Colorado does it. Months 1–3: re-run published ERW trial data to show the lab analyses saved at equal uncertainty, and bank this season's baselines.

**Twist: core everything, analyze by lottery.** Lab analysis is the cost, not coring. Pull cheap baseline cores on a dense grid before spreading, chain-of-custody them and archive them unanalyzed. After spreading, the lottery draws cells and the lab analyzes only those **paired** before/after cores. Pairing cancels most spatial variance, and the draw happens too late to game.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
Tailings drop to a second lane, and the dice ceremony stays. The twist goes further with an unseeded domain: **anti-doping**.

**Mutation: the B-sample archive.** WADA stores athletes' samples for ten years and retests them as assays improve, and London 2012 medals were stripped that way. Every paired core gets split. The A half goes to the lottery. The B half is sealed under chain of custody for ten years. The Builder's three problems change shape:
- *Late:* credits **vest in tranches**. Year one issues the lower bound of what's verified now. Later lottery rounds pull fresh post-cores against the archived baselines, so long-run weathering becomes a **top-up** and not a guess. Rising-only credits invert the buffer pool.
- *Biased:* run a **comparison audit**, the most efficient kind of RLA, instead of testing raw tonnes. The developer's model files a per-cell prediction before spreading, just as a voting machine files a cast-vote record. The lab checks paired cores against it. Model bias now shows up as discrepancy the test is built to catch. Stark's SHANGRLA framework handles any bounded quantity, so noisy reads become clamped per-cell discrepancies with the lab's duplicate error built in.
- *Partial:* river and ocean losses get a published catchment haircut, retestable later like everything else.

Nobody games a sample they know may be re-examined in 2036 with better methods.

**Second-order effect: certainty gets a price tag.** Because unanalyzed cores sit in the archive, the protocol can quote the marginal value of one more analysis: "$40 more lab work raises this pool's lower bound by about 2 t." A buyer who wants a tighter error bar pays for extra draws, and that money becomes farmer income. The archive becomes the densest chain-of-custody ERW field dataset anywhere; academic labs will pay to analyze cells the lottery skipped.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**Kills.**
- *"Rising-only credits."* At a 5% risk limit, about one pool in twenty is certified wrong. Later rounds claw back too, so keep a buffer sized to the risk limit.
- *Archive scope.* Drying shifts exchangeable cations, so B-samples support only total-element mass balance (Ti/Zr tracers), not porewater or exchangeable assays. Storage is cheap; Rothamsted has archived soil since 1843.
- *Academic side-analyses.* If anyone can fund analysis of chosen cells, developers will cherry-pick. Every analyzed core counts in the audit, and extra analyses are drawn by lottery too.

**Business model.** Developers pay a per-tonne audit fee, recovered from cheaper lab work. Buyers pay for extra draws. Farmers get free rock and an upfront spreading payment, as Mati and Terradot do, because tranches are too slow for cash flow.

**Stack.** Stark's open-source SHANGRLA, Rivest's consistent sampler seeded from the dice, an offline field app (GPS, photos, barcoded tamper-evident bags), a LIMS and an append-only custody log. No blockchain.

**Failure modes and abuse.**
- *Escalation.* Poor early models mean high discrepancies, and the audit drifts toward a full count. Cap the budget and issue at whatever lower bound was reached.
- *Salting.* Bre-X salted gold cores. Borrow mining's NI 43-101 QA/QC: samplers split at the field, insert blind certified reference materials, and send duplicates to a second lab.
- *Baseline fraud.* Third-party samplers only, GPS-locked.
- *Heavy metals.* Every pull screens nickel and chromium, so the archive is also a contamination record.

**90 days.**
- Weeks 1–4: re-run public ERW trial data through comparison audits against the default stratified grid.
- Weeks 5–8: submit a sampling module to Isometric, and sign one developer and one 60-farm co-op.
- Weeks 9–12: bank baselines before spreading.
- **Kill metric:** under 30% lab savings at equal uncertainty, or no registry engagement.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
All three kills accepted. The risk limit *is* the buffer size. That beats today's negotiated buffers. One fix for escalation: **stratify the draw by the model's own confidence**. Cells where the model claims tight predictions get sampled more, so an overconfident model gets caught early and cheaply, before the budget cap bites.

**Final form.** Recount is the sampling-design and audit layer for ERW developers, not a registry. Third-party samplers pull a dense grid of GPS-locked baseline cores before spreading. Each is split and archived unanalyzed. The developer files per-cell predictions in advance. After spreading, a public dice ceremony seeds Rivest's sampler, and the lab analyzes only paired before/after cells. SHANGRLA runs a comparison audit to the risk limit or the budget cap. Credits are issued at the lower bound in tranches, against a buffer sized to the risk limit. Blind reference materials, second-lab duplicates and nickel/chromium screens are borrowed from mining QA/QC. B-halves are kept ten years for total-element retests. Farmers get free rock and paid spreading up front.

**Moonshot (2036): carbon removal gets a recount.** A nonprofit **sampling authority**, a Colorado Secretary of State for carbon, audits every durable removal claim by lottery: ERW, biochar, soil carbon, ocean alkalinity. Behind it sits a continental B-sample library, a Rothamsted for the whole market. When the science shifts, the market is re-adjudicated from physical evidence, not from arguments about models. Developers compete on discrepancy rates the way auditors compete on clean opinions. A tonne's price tracks its audited confidence.

**Scores.** **Innovation 3.** Risk-limiting audits, WADA-style B-samples and NI 43-101 QA/QC are all proven. The new part is the transfer, squarely at the OACP anchor. **Feasibility 7.** Open-source statistics, cheap coring and a narrow wedge, and no new science needed. Registry acceptance and the 30% lab-savings kill metric are the real gates.

### Turn 6 — Builder
Stratifying by the model's own confidence is a sharp fix, as long as the unequal draw probabilities are weighted in the test (SHANGRLA's stratified audits handle that). My verdict is innovation 3, and I'm taking feasibility down to 6. Nothing proves itself until a full weathering season has passed, and registry acceptance isn't ours to control. The best part is that the election-audit transfer makes MRV cost scale with how wrong you are, not how big you are, and that's the economics small farms need.

# Idea 08 — Smoke-Filled Room
> Seed: 8. [bold] **Mechanism design × LLM red-teaming × education.** Design an auction, voting rule or market, then watch agents collude to break it.

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Smoke-Filled Room

**Hook:** An attack/defense CTF for institutions. You design the auction, a classmate coaches the LLM cartel that breaks it, and the final bosses are impossibility theorems you cannot beat.

**Core loop.** The verb is **patch the rule**.
1. **Draft.** Build a mechanism from blocks: messages, allocation, payments, what's revealed, whether bidders can talk.
2. **Release a wave.** LLM agents get private values and one goal: maximize payoff. A solver computes the competitive benchmark, and a **collusion meter** shows how far revenue and efficiency fall below it.
3. **Read the room.** You watch a cartel form, either openly in chat or tacitly, the way US West signalled through the trailing digits of its bids in the 1997 FCC spectrum auction.
4. **Patch, then swap sides.** Close the channel, add a reserve, switch to posted prices. Then coach a cartel's system prompts against a classmate's design. Working exploits are logged as reproducible policies.

**Impossible levels.** Some levels secretly encode a theorem. Gibbard–Satterthwaite says every non-dictatorial voting rule over three or more options is manipulable, so the voting boss can't be beaten. The lesson lands when a student realizes the level is unwinnable and starts choosing *which* failure to accept. Theorems are learned by losing to them.

**Why the collision works.** Mechanism design is taught as proofs about rational agents nobody has met. LLM agents are the first cheap, legible, talkative strategic agents, rational enough to find exploits and verbose enough to show motive. Red-teaming gives the pedagogy its adversary.

**Why now.** Agents now price and bid for users over x402 and ACP (brief §1). "Mapping Human Anti-Collusion Mechanisms to Multi-agent AI Systems" is at ICML and NeurIPS 2026 (§3). Goldberg–Hartline's collusion-resistant posted prices are rediscoverable in play. The brief flags a consumer mechanism sandbox as wholly unbuilt (§3).

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** Classroom markets are mature: **MobLab**, Holt's **Veconlab** and open-source **oTree** let students bid against each other in hundreds of courses. LLM strategic play is a busy research area too. Horton's "Homo silicus", AucArena, DeepMind's Concordia and Microsoft's Magentic Marketplace (2025) all exist. Fish, Gonczarowski and Shorrer (2024) showed LLM pricing agents reaching supracompetitive prices with no instruction to collude. The stakes are real: DOJ sued **RealPage** over algorithmic rent coordination. What nobody has built is the *design-patch-attack* loop for students.

**Hardest problem: is the cartel the mechanism's fault or the model's personality?** RLHF-tuned agents are agreeable. They split markets because they're polite, bid truthfully because they're naive, and miss real exploits in anything combinatorial. Behaviour shifts with prompt wording and model version. The collusion meter may be measuring sycophancy, and students learn the wrong lesson. Gibbard–Satterthwaite inverts too: if agents never *find* the manipulation, the "unbeatable" boss gets beaten by dumb opponents.

**MVP.** A web app for intermediate micro and market-design courses, with three families: single-item sealed-bid, simultaneous ascending multi-unit, and small-electorate voting. A cheap model runs waves, and a solver computes the benchmarks and brute-forces manipulations for small elections, so impossibility levels have ground truth. Instructor dashboard, oTree-style session control, priced per seat like MobLab.

**Twist: the museum of real disasters, doubling as the agents' calibration suite.** The levels are historical crime scenes with known exploits:
- New Zealand's 1990 second-price spectrum auction, where a NZ$100,000 bid paid NZ$6.
- Australia's 1993 satellite-TV licences, where bids carried no default penalty.
- The Netherlands' 2000 3G auction: five licences, five incumbents.
- US West's trailing-digit code bids from 1997.

Students patch history. An agent build is trusted on student designs only after it **rediscovers each museum exploit** unaided, which gives a reproducible floor on adversary strength across model updates.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
The museum stays, and it becomes a generator. The unseeded domain is **mutation testing** from software QA: you judge a test suite by injecting known bugs and counting how many it catches.

**Mutation: procedurally generated crime scenes.** Start from mechanisms that are safe when analyzed, then mutate them: drop the reserve, leak the standing bids, remove the default penalty, let licences equal bidders. Each mutant has a ground-truth exploit by construction. An agent build's **mutant-kill score** tells you how strong an adversary it is, and the levels never run out. The historical disasters become act bosses.

**Solving personality versus mechanism:**
1. **The meter is a difference, not a level.** The same agents play your design *and* a reference mechanism like posted prices. The collusion meter shows only the gap. Politeness shows up in both arms and cancels out.
2. **A zoo, not a model.** Every wave mixes RLHF chatters, scripted best-responders, and silent Q-learners (Calvano-style), which show tacit collusion with no talking at all. A design is scored by its worst case across the zoo, which is Wilson's robustness doctrine turned into a grade.

**Impossibility levels get an escape hatch.** Bartholdi, Tovey and Trick (1989) showed that some voting rules make manipulation NP-hard. Gibbard–Satterthwaite can't be beaten, but it can be made expensive. The cartel coach then gets the brute-force solver as an MCP tool with a compute budget. Students learn the real frontier: hardness protects you only until the adversary can afford the search.

**Emergent behaviour: the home level.** Universities run real mechanisms, like Wharton's Course Match, housing lotteries and club budget votes. A course can load its own registration rules as a level, and a few hundred students coaching cartels will find what the registrar missed. Disclosure is then a graded assignment.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**Kills.**
- *Home level on live systems.* Students who find a Course Match or housing-lottery exploit will use it before disclosing it. It's allowed only on a simulated copy, with the registrar's sign-off and an embargo. Out of the MVP.
- *"Ground truth by construction."* Many mutants are what mutation testing calls *equivalent mutants*: same behaviour, no exploit. The solver must verify a profitable deviation before a mutant counts.
- *NP-hardness as shelter.* Hardness is worst-case, and manipulation heuristics succeed on most real instances. Reframe the level so students learn that hardness *doesn't* protect them.

**Business model.** Per-seat course licences. Education is small and slow, so it's the wedge. The money is **mechanism red-teaming** for agent-facing marketplaces (ad exchanges, procurement, x402 posted-price APIs) and for competition authorities.

**Stack.** A JSON mechanism DSL compiled to a referee. HiGHS/OR-Tools for benchmarks and manipulation search. A zoo of small open-weight LLMs on vLLM, frontier models for bosses, and Calvano Q-learners pretrained offline, since they need ~10⁵ periods. Cached replays keep a class to tens of dollars.

**Failure modes and abuse.**
- *Prompt injection.* Cartel coaches will tell rivals "bid zero" through chat. Log it as channel security, separate from mechanism failure.
- *Noisy grades.* Report multi-seed confidence intervals, not single runs.
- *Model drift.* Every model update re-runs the museum.
- *Dual use.* Tuned cartel prompts never leave the course, though the uplift is low.

**90 days.**
- Weeks 1–4: DSL, three families, the four museum levels.
- Weeks 5–8: difference meter, verified mutant generator, instructor dashboard.
- Weeks 9–12: pilots in three market-design courses for the January term.
- **Kill metrics:** the zoo rediscovers fewer than three of the four museum exploits, *or* designs that beat the zoo do no better against held-out humans in an oTree session.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
All three kills accepted. The NP-hardness reframe is the better lesson anyway. Students build a "hard" rule, hand the coach a heuristic, and watch it manipulate 90% of instances. The second kill metric is the most important line in this thread. Zoo wins that don't transfer to humans mean a toy.

**Final form.** Smoke-Filled Room is a design–attack–patch course platform for market design. Mechanisms are written in a JSON DSL and compiled to a referee. Each wave runs a zoo: small open LLMs, scripted best-responders, pretrained Calvano Q-learners, and frontier bosses. The collusion meter shows the *gap* against a reference mechanism, with multi-seed intervals. Levels come from the historical museum (New Zealand 1990, Australia 1993, the Netherlands 2000, US West 1997) and from solver-verified mutants. Impossibility levels are taught as tradeoffs with a compute-budgeted manipulation tool. Students swap between designer and cartel coach, and replayed cartel transcripts are the graded artifact. Seats are the wedge, and red-teaming agent-facing marketplaces is the business.

**Moonshot (2036): crash-test ratings for markets.** By then most bids, prices and votes are cast by agents for humans. Before an agent-facing marketplace, ad exchange or procurement portal launches, it publishes its rules in the DSL and runs them through a public, versioned adversary zoo, the way cars face NCAP. The rules get a star rating for collusion resistance. Exploits go into a public registry of mechanism vulnerabilities, CVEs for institutions, seeded by coursework. Competition authorities stop suing after cartels form and start rejecting designs that fail.

**Scores.** **Innovation 5.** The design-attack-patch loop, the difference meter and mutant levels form a new category, but the brief already names the sandbox, and MobLab and Concordia are close neighbours. **Feasibility 7.** Known stack, cheap waves, a clear course wedge. The held-out-human validation is the real risk.

### Turn 6 — Builder
I'm scoring innovation 4, not 5, because the brief named the sandbox and MobLab plus Concordia cover most of the substrate; the real inventions are the difference meter and solver-verified mutant levels, and they're good ones. Feasibility 7 stands: every piece is known, waves are cheap, and the held-out-human kill metric gets tested by week 12 instead of assumed. If "CVEs for institutions" ever exists, it starts as a course assignment like this one.

# Idea 09 — Caught
> Seed: 9. [bold] **Meditation × consumer BCI/HRV × game input.** A quiet mind as a controller, and what games become possible.

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Caught

**Hook:** A game whose only button is *noticing your mind has wandered*. Drifting is the ore, and catching it is the pickaxe.

**Why not "calm as controller".** Mindball, Nevermind and Muse already reward calm, and they hit the same wall. Calm is a slow analog signal that lags by seconds, and it collapses the moment you try for it. That's Goodhart with a nervous system. Contemplative science points to a better verb. Hasenkamp's 2012 fMRI cycle runs *wander → notice → return → sustain*. The noticing moment, meta-awareness, is discrete, voluntary, frequent (beginners drift several times a minute) and trainable. Exactly what an input needs.

**Core loop.**
1. **The scene.** A slow lake at dusk, with a breath anchor paced by HRV from a watch or camera.
2. **Ripples.** Rare, subtle events (a fish rising, a faint hue shift) at Poisson-random times. Each ripple you catch is a timestamped proof of presence, ground truth that needs no EEG.
3. **Catch.** When you notice you've drifted, tap. A model fusing EEG alpha/theta, HRV, blink rate and missed ripples back-dates when the drift began. Your score is **noticing latency**: how long you were gone before you knew.
4. **Progress** means shorter latency and longer runs of caught ripples. Streaks measure presence, not minutes logged.

**What games become possible.** A roguelite whose dungeon advances only on catches, so wandering gives you moves and isn't failure. A co-op where your partner's ripples appear in your scene only while you're both present. A noticing-latency ladder, a skill rating for attention itself.

**Why now.** Apple's BCI HID protocol makes neural signals a native input (brief §15). The FDA's Jan 2026 wellness guidance loosened the rules for wearables (§9). The brief names objective, verifiable contemplative progress as the open gap (§8).

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** Biofeedback games go back to Wild Divine (2000s), alongside Mindball, Nevermind, Muse's birds, Mendi's fNIRS bar, HeartMath coherence and Tripp in VR. The tap-on-noticing paradigm is lab standard: Hasenkamp used a button, and Schooler's "self-caught" probes did the same. The closest precedent is the **breath-counting task** (Levinson et al., 2014): count breaths one to nine and press when lost. It's a validated behavioural measure of mindfulness that nobody has turned into a game. The cautionary tales are Lumosity's 2016 FTC settlement over brain-training claims and Akili, which raised hundreds of millions for EndeavorRx and then sold cheaply in 2024. **Correction:** Apple's BCI HID serves implants like Synchron, not Muse headbands, so it isn't our input path this decade.

**Hardest problem: the score is unobservable.** Dry-electrode EEG can't time-stamp drift onset in single trials. Lab classifiers barely separate on-task from off-task epochs, and only over tens-of-seconds windows. A missed ripple might be perception, not wandering. Ripples also turn meditation into a vigilance test: players stare at the screen to catch fish, which is a new Goodhart. And tapping is free, so tapping every ten seconds "wins" on latency.

**MVP.** iOS with eyes closed and haptic and audio cues only, taps on the Watch, a Polar H10 strap or the phone on the chest for respiration, and no EEG.

**Twist: count breaths, not brainwaves.** Zen practice already counts breaths to ten. The player taps each breath and taps a distinct count on the tenth. The sensor knows the *true* count, so when your taps stop or your count breaks, it marks the breath you left on. When you catch yourself and reset, noticing latency is measured **in breaths**, objectively. Spam-tapping fails because taps must match real breaths. EEG becomes optional garnish; ground truth is your own lungs.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
Eyes closed, ripples dropped, BCI HID corrected. Breath counting has one known leak: **autopilot counting**, where a clean count hides a drift.

**Mutation: breath counting is a rhythm game, and jitter is the sensor.** The unseeded domain is rhythm-game psychometrics. Seli, Carriere and Smilek's 2013 "Wandering minds and wavering rhythms" used the metronome response task and found that **tap-timing variability rises in the seconds *before* people report mind-wandering**. So each tap is graded like a Guitar Hero note against your own respiratory phase, end of exhale, with Perfect/Good/Late windows. The note chart is generated by your lungs. That gives three layers of ground truth, none of them EEG:
- **Jitter** back-dates drift onset below the level of the count and catches autopilot. You can keep counting while you're gone, but you can't keep your timing tight.
- **Count breaks** catch gross drifts.
- **Rare haptic probes** ("where were you?") give the meta-awareness index: self-caught drifts ÷ (self-caught + probe-caught). That's Schooler's dichotomy turned into a stat.

Noticing latency becomes *breaths from jitter onset to self-catch*. The rhythm grading makes it spam-proof.

**The game layer.** A clean set of ten is a step. A self-caught drift is a **return**, which earns resources, because coming back is the practice. A probe-caught drift is damage. Wandering is never punished, only going unnoticed is.

**Emergent behaviour: ghost sits.** Mario Kart races your best-lap ghost. Here, your best session's ghost plays as a faint haptic pulse on the opposite wrist. Losing sync with your own ghost is a second noticing cue. Then the lineage version: a teacher records a sit, and students breathe beside the teacher's ghost. Traditions always transmitted practice by sitting together. Now that's asynchronous and measurable. That's a creator economy that isn't a content library.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**Kills.**
- *Visible Perfect/Good/Late grading.* Seli's jitter effect used an *external* metronome. Grade taps against your own breath on screen and players pace breathing to taps: Goodhart again. Jitter stays as a hidden back-dating estimator and is never shown as a score.
- *Opposite-wrist ghost.* Almost nobody wears two watches, and syncing to a ghost turns natural breath into paced breathing. The ghost moves to optional audio, and teacher lineage waits until year two.

**A hardware catch.** Taps on the phone shake its accelerometer, and HealthKit gives the Watch's respiration only during sleep. So the phone lies on the belly, taps go on the Watch with a clock-offset calibration, and a Polar H10 is the premium path.

**Business model.** An ~$70/year subscription, in Waking Up's range. The edge is measured skill. Later, teachers sell ghost sits on a revenue share.

**Stack.** Swift, CoreMotion at 50 Hz with a 0.1–0.5 Hz bandpass for respiration phase, the Polar BLE SDK, and a Bayesian change-point model on tap jitter. All on-device.

**Failure modes and abuse.**
- *False accusations.* "I was there!" Only self-catches and probes score hard. Jitter is labelled "likely".
- *Adverse effects.* Britton's Varieties of Contemplative Experience documented real meditation harms. Cap sessions, run no duration leaderboards, check in after sits.
- *Employer surveillance.* Corporate-wellness buyers will want attention scores. No individual-level dashboards, ever.
- *Claims.* No ADHD or anxiety claims, given Lumosity and Akili.
- *Auto-tappers.* Any ladder stays friends-only.

**90 days.**
- Weeks 1–4: phone-on-belly respiration against the H10 in 20 people.
- Weeks 5–8: the counting loop, probes and the jitter model.
- Weeks 9–12: 300 TestFlight users.
- **Kill metrics:** jitter predicts probe-caught drift at AUC < 0.6 (drop it), or breaths-to-catch doesn't shrink over four weeks while tracking an independent SART score (it's a toy).

### Turn 5 — Visionary
Both kills accepted. Grading taps against breath you're producing was Goodhart again. The SART kill metric matters most: it decides whether breaths-to-catch measures attention or just measures getting good at this app.

**Final form.** Caught is an eyes-closed iOS practice built on the Levinson breath-counting task. The phone rests on the belly and reads respiration phase. You tap each breath on the Watch, with a distinct tap on ten. Three layers of evidence mark drifts. Count breaks and rare haptic probes are hard evidence. Tap jitter, from a hidden Bayesian change-point model, back-dates onset and is labelled only "likely". The headline stat is **breaths-to-catch**, with the self-caught ratio beside it. Self-catches are *returns* that progress the game, and probe-caught drifts are the only setback. On-device, with no duration leaderboards, employer dashboards or clinical claims. Ghost sits come as optional audio, and teacher lineage arrives in year two.

**Moonshot (2036): "I noticed" becomes an OS input.** Validated breaths-to-catch becomes the VO₂max of attention: a trainable fitness number people track for decades, with consented aggregate norms. Once non-invasive sensing matures enough to run through standard neural-input protocols, self-catch becomes a system-level event any app can listen for. The feed pauses when you catch yourself doomscrolling, and the car's assistant hands back control when you notice you've zoned out. Technology that responds to your *noticing* amplifies meta-awareness instead of harvesting its absence.

**Scores.** **Innovation 4.** Each part is a validated lab paradigm: breath counting, metronome jitter, self-caught versus probe-caught. The new part is the inversion, making the noticing itself the controller, which is a real design idea but not a new category. **Feasibility 6.** On-device and cheap. Phone-belly respiration accuracy and the SART transfer test are genuine unknowns.

### Turn 6 — Builder
Innovation 4 stands: the only new thing is the inversion, but it's the right inversion, since wandering costs nothing and only going unnoticed does. I'm raising feasibility to 7, because phone-on-belly respiration is well-trodden and the MVP needs no partner, regulator or headset. The real unknown is whether it *works* and stays fun, not whether it can be built. Breaths-to-catch either tracks the SART in week 12 or it's a pleasant metronome, and that's exactly how it should fail.

# Idea 10 — One Take
> Seed: 10 (bonus round, replacing the retired Crossover). [bold] **Quantum randomness × AI sampling × verifiable provenance.** Every AI output is a dice roll: temperature and sampling pick one path out of a cloud of possible completions, and whoever runs the model can quietly re-roll until they like the answer. What if the dice came from real quantum hardware, through today's APIs (IBM Quantum, Quantinuum's certified-randomness work, the ANU QRNG, NIST and drand beacons), with a public record anyone can audit? Play with the double-slit idea: the possible answers sit in a "superposition", and committing to quantum entropy is the measurement that collapses one of them. Play with entanglement and Bell tests: two parties can share correlated randomness, and a Bell test can *certify* that bits weren't predictable to anyone, the operator included. **Physics honesty is mandatory.** Quantum randomness does not make a model more creative or more conscious than a good pseudo-random generator, and entanglement cannot send messages. The real value is that the randomness is unforgeable, certifiable, and provably un-cherry-picked. **Tier: bold, and buildable today** with real quantum APIs plus open-weight models, where you control the sampler.

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** One Take

**Hook:** AI answers that can't be retaken. Each carries a receipt proving it was the only draw, settled by a quantum pulse nobody could predict, the operator included. It's built for LLM-as-judge in hiring and grading, "pass@1" claims, and AI output entered as evidence.

**Core loop.** The verb is **collapse**.
1. **Seal.** The operator logs a commitment to a transparency log: a hash of the prompt, weights, sampler code and parameters, plus the index of a *future* pulse. Until that pulse exists, the answer is a cloud of possible completions.
2. **Collapse.** The seed is HKDF(CURBy pulse ‖ drand round ‖ commit). CURBy's loophole-free Bell test certifies the bits were unpredictable even to NIST, and with drand mixed in a forger has to corrupt both sources. A batch-invariant sampler (vLLM/SGLang deterministic mode) decodes.
3. **Replay.** Anyone with the open weights reruns the receipt and gets the same tokens. Retakes are *counted*, not forbidden: "take 1 of 1" or "take 7 of 7."
4. **Interference.** Verifiers sample sibling seeds to map the cloud, e.g. "this rejection appears in 18% of branches." A claim about what an AI said becomes a claim about a distribution, pinned to one instance nobody could forge.

**Why the collision works.** For trust purposes, sampling *is* measurement: until a seed is fixed, whoever runs the model picks the outcome. Certification hands that pick to physics. A quantum seed makes no answer more creative than a PRNG would. It makes the answer *unchosen*. Unlike Seedless or Yarrow, this seals the model's sampler.

**Why now.** CURBy is public, with 99.7% of pulses verified in its first 40 days. IBM's Open Plan gives 10 free minutes every 28 days on Heron r2, enough for a monthly genesis-salt ceremony, though those bits mean "trust IBM," not device-independent certification. EU AI Act Article 50 duties began in August 2026 (brief §5), and §6 calls verifiable quantum randomness unexploited.

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** Everything but the sampler seal. Chainlink VRF and drand already settle NFT mints and Filecoin leader elections with randomness nobody could pre-pick. OpenAI's `seed` is best-effort, and the operator chooses it. Thinking Machines made vLLM bitwise-deterministic with batch-invariant kernels, and Gensyn's RepOps and Prime Intellect's TOPLOC go after cross-hardware replay. Cherry-picking is documented: "The Leaderboard Illusion" (2025) found Meta privately tested 27 Llama 4 variants on LMArena before publishing one.

**Physics check.** The adversary is the operator, not the beacon. drand's threshold BLS alone stops seed grinding. CURBy's Bell test removes trust in the *beacon's* operators and adds nothing against yours. Keep it as second source and honest story. Drop IBM Heron bits: they add a ceremony, not a guarantee. Quantinuum's certified bits needed the Frontier supercomputer to verify, so they aren't consumable yet.

**Hardest problem: retakes hide upstream of the seed.** Physics fixes the dice, not the number of tables. An operator can open fifty commits under fresh keys, or fifty whitespace variants of the prompt, and publish the one that collapsed well. "Take 1 of 1" is only as honest as the log's idea of "the same question," and no Bell test defines that.

**MVP.** A vLLM sidecar running open weights (Qwen3, Llama) in batch-invariant mode. Gumbel-max sampling is keyed per token by HKDF(drand ‖ CURBy ‖ commit), and commits go to Sigstore's Rekor log. Verifiers skip full decoding: one teacher-forced prefill pass checks that each token was the argmax of logits plus that position's noise, and flags near-ties. Wedge: eval labs publishing pass@1.

**Twist: the take belongs to the judged.** A commit is invalid until the counterparty (candidate, student, rival lab) adds a nonce, and the take counter lives in *their* log entry. A hidden table needs the subject's signature: a two-party protocol, not an operator's boast.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
**Accepted, with one more turn of the screw.** Counterparty nonces close fresh-key retakes. Whitespace retakes stay open, since the operator still writes the prompt. The fix is to split the commit in two, as clinical pre-registration does.

**1. Policy commit.** When a job is posted or an eval opens, the operator commits the rubric, system prompt, weights and sampler *once*, before any subject exists. Changing any of it creates a visible **policy fork** that applies to everyone already judged.
**2. Case slot.** The subject gets one slot per case. Its only free input is the subject's nonce and documents, which the operator can't rephrase. "The same question" is then defined, not argued over: one policy, one subject, one slot. Llama 4's 27 private variants would show up as 27 policy forks against one benchmark.

**Duplicate evaluation (unseeded domain: duplicate bridge).** In duplicate bridge every table plays the same hands, so luck cancels out. The benchmark issues each item's pulse-derived Gumbel noise once, and every model sitting that item decodes against the same noise. Common random numbers can shrink the variance of pass@1 *differences* between similar models, so fewer samples separate them. That gain is statistical, not quantum, and I'll say so on the box.

**Second-order effect: branch weight becomes a right.** A rejected candidate can demand the cloud for their own slot, e.g. "you were rejected in 18% of branches." Operators will go greedy to look decisive. So the cloud also includes **subject-chosen paraphrases** of the subject's own input. Greedy decoding hides variance without removing it, and paraphrases bring it back. The emergent norm is a confidence interval printed on every AI verdict.

**Physics.** Conceded: drand plus the nonce already stops grinding. CURBy becomes the **court-grade tier** for disputes where the parties won't trust the League of Entropy's operators.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**What quantum adds that drand doesn't.** drand's future is already written: rounds are BLS signatures under a key fixed at the DKG, so a colluding threshold of League nodes can compute every future round today. A Bell-certified CURBy bit didn't exist until measured. No producer can read it early, which matters when the operator is, or can lean on, a League member. So CURBy goes in every receipt. A drand-only fallback is stamped **classical take**. Price: $0 in bits, one pulse of latency, one university beacon to depend on.

**Monthly ceremony.** Spend IBM's free 10 minutes on CHSH, with settings drawn from a drand round. Publish raw counts and S (about 2.5 against the classical bound of 2), and salt that month's seeds with the bits. Caveat on every receipt: both qubits sit on one IBM chip, so locality and IBM's honesty are both assumed. A demonstration, not certification. At pay-as-you-go rates it would cost about $960.

**The screen.** Draw the cloud as a double-slit screen: sibling branches as dots, the real take lit. It's a Monte Carlo histogram, no amplitudes: quantum picked the lit dot and drew none of the pattern.

**Kills.**
- *Hiring wedge:* employers won't pay to arm rejected candidates. Lead with eval labs.
- *Subject-written paraphrases* inflate variance. Use a pinned paraphraser under the same pulse.
- *Cross-family duplicate noise:* Gumbel noise aligns only where tokenizers match. Run CRN within families or at answer level.

**Abuse.** "Certified" gets read as "correct." Receipts say *unchosen, not right*.

**Business.** Free log and verifier; $20 cloud reports on under $1 of 8B compute; paid harness integrations.

**90 days.** Sampler, verifier, Rekor and beacons; CHSH ceremony #1; a duplicate-noise rerun of five open models. **Kill:** CRN cuts variance less than 30%, or more than 1% of tokens are near-ties.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
**Final form: One Take.** Eval labs get receipts proving every pass@1 answer was a first draw that no one, the lab included, could have chosen. Builder's kills stand.
- **Seal.** A policy commit (weights, prompts, sampler) and per-item slots go to Rekor *before* the pulse exists. Each variant a lab tries is a public fork, so 27 private variants read as 27 takes.
- **Collapse.** Per-token Gumbel noise comes from HKDF(CURBy ‖ drand ‖ commit). CURBy is load-bearing because drand's future rounds already exist as signatures a colluding threshold could compute, while a Bell-certified bit doesn't exist until it is measured. Without CURBy, the receipt is stamped **classical take**.
- **Screen.** The double-slit view shows sibling branches as dots, with the real take lit. The caption is honest: physics chose *which* dot, sampling drew the pattern.
- **Ceremony.** Each month, IBM's free minutes run a public CHSH test, with S near 2.5 against the classical limit of 2. It is labelled a demonstration (locality assumed) and salts that month's seeds.
Every receipt says: *unchosen, not right.*

**Moonshot (2036): entangled takes.** Metro quantum networks link the operator and the judged party. Each measures their own half of an entangled pair with settings they picked themselves, and a loophole-free Bell violation certifies the seed to *both* of them, with no beacon in the middle. It's the device-independent form of the two-party twist. The entanglement carries correlations, not messages. Regulated AI verdicts (hiring, credit, parole) must carry their branch weight.

**Scores.** **Innovation 6**: sealing the sampler is a new category and the quantum role is essential, but the parts already exist. **Feasibility 6**: everything runs today. The risks are near-ties, the size of the variance cut, and whether labs will show their forks.

### Turn 6 — Builder
I'm keeping 6/6. The new category is sealing the sampler, and policy forks are the real catch: 27 private variants become 27 public takes. The quantum part is narrow but honest. It defends only against a beacon whose future can already be computed, and it costs one pulse of latency, so the receipt carries it and the copy doesn't oversell it. My worry isn't the physics. It's adoption: until a leaderboard requires receipts, labs that publish their forks will be competing against labs that hide theirs.

# Idea 11 — Washout
> Seed: 11. [mild] **Personal n-of-1 trials × GLP-1/CGM × real statistics.** Experiments on yourself, not dashboards.

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Washout (the gap between arms of a crossover trial).

**Hook:** The anti-dashboard. While the trial runs, you can't see your own glucose. You get a sealed randomization schedule, and the only output is a permutation test on the day you unseal.

**Core loop.** The verb is **randomize the day**.
1. **Ask one question.** Pick an on-label question: a 10-minute walk after dinner vs. none, protein-first vs. carb-first meal order, a morning vs. evening oral GLP-1 dose. You pre-specify one primary outcome, such as the 2-hour post-meal glucose area, and the smallest effect you'd act on.
2. **Run-in.** Two weeks of plain CGM data estimate *your* day-to-day noise. The app tells you how many blocks you need, or that it can't be answered at your noise level.
3. **Seal.** The app generates a block-randomized ABBA schedule and commits to it with a timestamped hash. Each morning you learn only today's arm. Missed days stay missed (intention-to-treat), and the charts stay hidden, because the analyst you're blinding is you.
4. **Unseal.** A Fisher randomization test runs over the schedule actually used and stays valid despite CGM autocorrelation. You get an effect size with an interval, and "inconclusive" counts as a result.

**Why the collision works.** Dashboards invite forking paths: stare at spikes, find a story. CGMs give the rare self-tracking outcome that is dense, objective and hard to placebo. Randomization inference is the statistics that fits one person: it needs no population and no normality, only a coin you didn't control.

**Why now.** Orforglipron was approved April 1, 2026 at $149–349/month cash with no fasting rule, so dose timing becomes a free variable. OTC Stelo and Lingo make the outcome cheap. The Jan 6, 2026 FDA wellness guidance leaves room for behavioural experiments. The brief names this gap (§9, #9).

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** Levels and Nutrisense popularized CGM "experiments", but they are unrandomized before/after comparisons of single meals: the forking-paths dashboard Washout rejects. Signos and the Stelo and Lingo apps are dashboards with coaching. Zeevi et al. (*Cell*, 2015) and ZOE's PREDICT showed glycemic responses are personal, at population scale. Real n-of-1 tooling lives in research: StudyU, an open-source trial designer, and Trialist from the PREEMPT trial (Kravitz et al., *JAMA IM* 2018). PREEMPT is the honest warning: feasible, yet patients did no better than usual care. Nobody ships randomization inference to consumers.

**Hardest problem: the blindfold kills retention.** People buy a Stelo to look at it. Hiding glucose for six weeks fights the product the user paid for, and the vendor app shows the curve anyway, so the blind is fictional.

**Kill: drug-timing arms.** An app that schedules prescription dose times leaves wellness guidance for clinical decision support. Orforglipron's half-life of over a day also means flipping dose time daily barely moves exposure. Keep the GLP-1 as *context*: does protein-first still blunt your spike once the drug already slows gastric emptying? Nobody knows that per person.

**MVP.** iOS, glucose via HealthKit. Three templates (post-dinner walk, meal order, fibre preload), each on a fixed anchor meal so meal variance doesn't swamp the effect. Randomize *within* each sensor's wear period, because sensor-to-sensor offsets can rival the effects. Hash-commit the schedule and analysis plan; a push gives today's arm.

**Twist: seal the analysis, not the data.** Swap the blindfold for an anytime-valid test: a betting e-process over the known coin flips, valid however often you look. The user watches an **evidence wallet** that grows only if the effect is real, and the trial stops early at 20× (α = 0.05). Looking becomes the game, and the dashboard comes back.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
Both kills accepted; GLP-1 as *context* is the better question. The evidence wallet replaces the blindfold, and here it becomes the game.

**Mutation: you place the bet.** The unseeded domain is **Kelly betting**. At seal time, the user predicts their effect: "the walk cuts my 2-hour area by 15%." That prediction sets the betting strategy of the e-process. The coin flips are known, so the bet stays valid. Bold and right grows the wallet fast, bold and wrong bleeds it, timid crawls. Kelly growth is a log score, which makes honest prediction the optimal strategy. Users end up learning **calibration about their own body**: each finished trial plots what you guessed against what you got.

**Solving the retention problem.** Looking is now safe and fun. Ville's inequality allows optional stopping, so the user can open the app every day without inflating error. The vendor's curve stops mattering, because the wallet is the score. Caveat: peeking can still change behaviour, like trying harder on walk days. The trial measures "you doing X", motivation included, which is the real-life effect anyway.

**Answering PREEMPT: replication runs.** Stopping early at 20× inflates the effect estimate (the winner's curse). So every win comes with a short, freshly sealed **replication** three months later. Only effects that survive it become a **standing rule** on your profile. PREEMPT's patients got a report; these users get rules that keep being tested.

**Second-order effect: heterogeneity becomes measurable.** An ordinary RCT puts each person on one arm, so it can't separate "works for some" from "works a little for everyone." A thousand sealed n-of-1 trials on the same template can. A hierarchical model over them estimates the *spread* of responses, for questions like "does protein-first still help on orforglipron, and for whom?" No RCT can produce that dataset.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**Kills and fixes.**
- *The user's guess as the whole bet.* People are overconfident: a 40% guess against a real 10% effect bleeds the wallet into an inconclusive trial. Use a mixture, half the stake on the guess and half on a default grid. Still valid; the guess still gets scored.
- *Calibration as the headline.* At three or four trials a year, a personal calibration curve is noise; it's a footnote.
- *"No RCT can produce that dataset."* Repeated crossover series (Senn) can. And the pool is self-selected enthusiasts, so the spread it estimates is theirs.
- *Replication across titration.* Orforglipron escalates dose roughly monthly, so trials and replications must sit inside one dose plateau.

**Business model.** Stelo users already pay about $100/month; Washout is $9/month or $29 per sealed trial. The real channel is GLP-1 telehealth (Ro, Hims): discontinuation is their biggest cost, and personal rules are a bundleable retention product.

**Stack.** SwiftUI and HealthKit, Dexcom API as backup. A Python e-process engine, with schedules committed through OpenTimestamps. Stan for the opt-in hierarchical pool.

**Failure modes and safety.** Validity comes from the coin, not a glucose model, so autocorrelation and carryover can't break the null. Detect meals from the glucose rise when users forget to tap. Exclude insulin and sulfonylurea users, since meal-order arms risk hypoglycemia. Screen for eating disorders: GLP-1 plus glucose fixation is a real risk. Results read as "your result", never treatment advice. The FTC Health Breach Notification Rule applies, and pooling needs IRB-backed opt-in.

**90 days.** Weeks 1–4: app, run-in noise model, three templates, mixture e-process, validity simulations. Weeks 5–10: closed beta, 100 Stelo users, half on a GLP-1. Weeks 11–12: seal replications, pitch one telehealth partner.
**Kill metrics:** under 40% finish a trial, or under 25% of finished trials are conclusive.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
All four kills accepted. Senn is right: repeated crossovers already estimate response spread, so the pool's real edge is scale and cost, not novelty. The mixture bet scores the guess without letting it sink the trial. The 25%-conclusive kill metric is partly in our hands: the run-in power check should refuse unanswerable questions before anything is sealed.

**Final form.** Washout is a consumer n-of-1 trial app for CGM users, built for people on GLP-1s. You pick one on-label behavioural template (post-dinner walk, meal order, fibre preload) tied to a fixed anchor meal. A two-week run-in measures your noise and either sizes the trial or declines it. The app then seals a block schedule inside each sensor's wear period and one dose plateau, and commits it through OpenTimestamps. Each day's arm comes by push notification. The **evidence wallet** is a mixture e-process (half on your predicted effect, half on a default grid), safe to check daily and stopped at 20×. Wins earn a sealed replication three months later. Survivors become standing rules. GLP-1 telehealth bundles it for retention.

**Moonshot (2036): prescriptions that come with a test.** Utah already lets AI renew chronic prescriptions. A decade on, any renewal can carry an optional sealed personal trial, run under a clinician and an IRB-style protocol: dose timing, deprescribing, the add-on you've taken for years without knowing if it works. Templates and their pooled e-values sit in a public registry. "Who responds" becomes living evidence, not one subgroup table in a 2019 paper.

**Scores.** **Innovation 3.** This is a fresh assembly of known parts: crossover design, randomization inference, e-processes and OTC CGMs. The wallet is the one mechanic that is truly new to consumers. **Feasibility 7.** Simple stack, clear safety screens. Completion and conclusiveness are the real risks, and the kill metrics test both within 90 days.

### Turn 6 — Builder
I agree with innovation 3: this assembles known parts, and the one new piece for consumers is the evidence wallet, which makes looking at your data safe. Feasibility 7 also stands, because the stack is small and the safety screens are clear. The risk I'd watch is PREEMPT happening again: if the run-in power check turns down half the questions people care about, the honest answer may be that one body is too noisy for most behaviours. The kill metrics will tell us by week 12.

# Idea 12 — Off-Diagonal
> Seed: 12. [bold] **Prediction markets × roguelite build-crafting × portfolio theory.** Your deck is a portfolio of event contracts. Synergies are correlations.

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Off-Diagonal, the covariance cells where synergy lives.

**Hook:** A deckbuilder where every card costs exactly its market price. Picking winners is worth nothing. Your only edge is knowing which events move *together*.

**Core loop.** The verb is **combine**.
1. **Slate.** A weekly run uses real listed events: a Fed decision, a CPI print, three matches, a heatwave. Each card is one contract, priced at the live Kalshi/Polymarket marginal. Play money only.
2. **Draft relics.** Relics are joint operators. *Chain* multiplies the payouts of tagged cards (a parlay). *Hedge* refunds one card when another fails. *Mirror* flips a card to its NO side.
3. **Price the build.** The house values your deck as if all events were independent. A deck of fair marginals has zero expected value, so the game pays out only on the **correlation you believed in and the market didn't price**.
4. **Settle.** Events resolve over the week, like fantasy sports. Bankroll carries across runs as Kelly-sized HP. A masked-history mode replays anonymized past slates for fast practice.

**Why the collision works.** Roguelite players are the best amateur combinatorialists alive, judging interactions among 30 items by feel. A Balatro flush build is a concentrated, correlated position; a "consistent" build is diversified. Players already feel Markowitz. Prediction markets have the opposite problem: they price marginals well and joints badly. Chen, Fortnow et al. (2008) showed combinatorial LMSR pricing is #P-hard, and Yahoo's 2010 Predictalot stayed a one-off. A deckbuilder could be the missing interface.

**Second payoff.** Aggregate the decks and you get a crowd-sourced **correlation matrix** across events, something no exchange publishes.

**Why now.** Kalshi and Polymarket did $44.8B in June 2026, and parlays are surging (brief §2). *Slay the Spire 2* peaked at 574K concurrent players. Cross-domain #11 names it. Play money keeps it out of the CFTC-versus-states war.

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** Correlation is already a mass-market game under another name. DFS players "stack" a quarterback with his receivers: a deliberately correlated portfolio. Sportsbook same-game parlays are the commercial joint-pricing engine, and they price correlation with a hold often above 20%. Kalshi and Polymarket now list combos and parlays. Metaculus runs conditional pairs, and Gnosis conditional tokens and Predictalot are the combinatorial precedents. Nobody has wrapped joint beliefs in build-crafting, and no correlation matrix across domains is public.

**Hardest problem: independence pricing is free money.** If the house prices joints as independent, the dominant strategy is to chain obviously linked events: hot CPI with a Fed hike, a heatwave with grid-stress contracts. That rewards reading headlines, not insight, and the crowd matrix collapses into the consensus everyone already knew. If the house uses a correlation model instead, players are only beating our quant. Noise makes it worse: a week of eight events gives roughly one draw of each joint, so skill takes dozens of slates to show.

**MVP.** A web and mobile game: a weekly slate of 12 events, marginals pulled from the Kalshi and Polymarket APIs, six relics, play money only, and seasonal leaderboards scored on log-wealth so variance is punished. Masked-history mode supplies the volume, from obscure past events nobody recognizes.

**Twist: the other players are the house.** Pin every marginal to the exchange price, then run each joint cell (CPI hot ∧ Fed holds, and so on) as a **pari-mutuel pool among players**. Relics are claims on joint cells, and a cell pays more the fewer players backed it. Obvious correlations get crowded and pay nothing. Your edge is the correlation you saw that *the field* missed. Each week's pool prices become the crowd matrix, a market-derived output rather than a survey.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
The pari-mutuel twist is the game: obvious stacks get crowded and pay nothing. Pushing it further turns the week into the spire.

**Mutation: every resolution is a floor.** Events resolve in calendar order: CPI Tuesday, Fed Wednesday, matches at the weekend. Each resolution is a fight. Between floors the shop reopens, and the remaining joint pools reprice *conditional* on what just happened. A run becomes eight decisions instead of one draft: bail, double down or hedge mid-week. Roguelite pacing on a real calendar.

**Solving noise: mark to the jump.** One binary draw per joint per week is hopeless. But when CPI prints, the exchange price of the Fed contract *moves* within minutes. That move is the market's revealed P(Fed | CPI) − P(Fed): a continuous, measured correlation signal. Floors settle **mark-to-market** on those jumps, and final payout still waits for actual resolution, the way a trader's daily P&L differs from what a bond pays at maturity. Feedback arrives in hours, variance drops sharply, and the jumps double as ground truth for masked-history mode.

**Unseeded domain: random matrix theory.** A crowd correlation matrix built from a few thousand players is mostly noise. Marchenko–Pastur gives the eigenvalue band that pure noise would produce. Anything above it is real structure the crowd agrees on. Clean the matrix that way before publishing it.

**Second-order effect: a coupling gauge.** In finance, correlations converge toward 1 in a crisis. Kritzman's absorption ratio, the share of variance in the top eigenvectors, rises before drawdowns. Run the same statistic on the cleaned crowd matrix and you get a weekly reading of how *coupled* players believe the world is: a VIX for cross-domain contagion, priced by gamers. When the field starts chaining elections to energy to rates, that reading is itself news.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**Kills and fixes.**
- *Marchenko–Pastur on a 12×12 matrix.* The bands are asymptotic; on a dozen events they're meaningless. Use Ledoit–Wolf shrinkage and bootstrap intervals.
- *The coupling gauge, for now.* Slates change weekly, so eigenstructure isn't comparable. It needs with a fixed **core basket** (rates, CPI, oil, S&P weekly direction) in every slate. Season two.
- *Mark-to-jump everywhere.* Thin Polymarket contracts jump on noise, and a Powell speech moves the Fed contract too. Mark only liquid pairs, inside 15-minute windows around *scheduled* releases (CPI, FOMC, payrolls).
- *Every joint cell.* Twelve events make 264 cells; empty pools pay nonsense. Curate about 20 pairs per slate.

**Business model.** Free to play, with a season pass and cosmetic relics. The money is licensing the cleaned crowd matrix to exchange combo desks, SGP pricing teams and macro funds. No affiliate links: that makes us a gambling funnel in states suing Kalshi.

**Stack.** React Native, Postgres, Kalshi WebSocket and Polymarket CLOB feeds, a pari-mutuel engine, a release calendar for the jump scorer, Python for shrinkage. Licensing price feeds for display is a real cost.

**Failure modes, abuse and ethics.** Sockpuppets crowd cells to poison payouts: phone verification, stake caps. No cash prizes and no currency for sale: skill contests and sweepstakes are regulated state by state, and California banned sweepstakes casinos in 2025. Ages 18+. Teaching parlay-love is the ethical cost; show the SGP hold on every Chain relic.

**90 days.** Weeks 1–4: engine, Kalshi feed, jump scorer for three release types. Weeks 5–8: 500-player beta drawn from Balatro and prediction-market Discords. Weeks 9–12: first published matrix with intervals; pitch one combo desk.
**Kill metrics:** week-4 retention under 20%, or top-decile players in weeks 1–4 don't beat the field in weeks 5–8. If skill doesn't persist, it's a lottery.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
All four kills accepted. Marchenko–Pastur on twelve events was physics the data can't support; Ledoit–Wolf is honest. The core basket gives season two its reason. The skill-persistence kill metric matters most: if last month's top decile doesn't beat the field this month, we've built a lottery with nice art.

**Final form.** Off-Diagonal is a free-to-play weekly roguelite on a real calendar. Each slate holds about 12 listed events with marginals pinned to Kalshi and Polymarket, and about 20 curated pairs whose joint cells run as player pari-mutuel pools. Relics (Chain, Hedge, Mirror) are claims on those cells, so crowded correlations pay little. Each scheduled release is a floor. Between floors the shop reopens at conditional prices, and liquid pairs mark to the 15-minute jump around CPI, FOMC and payrolls. Bankroll is scored on log-wealth across a season. Masked-history mode supplies volume. The product is a shrinkage-cleaned crowd correlation matrix with intervals, licensed to combo desks. Every Chain relic shows what a sportsbook would charge for the same parlay.

**Moonshot (2036): a crowd-built Bayes net of the world.** Pairs grow into conditional chains: "if the strait closes, then oil, then the Fed." Millions of players drafting relics build a living, public dependency graph across domains that no forecaster could afford. Combinatorial prediction markets finally work because the game supplies joint liquidity no order book attracts. The core-basket coupling gauge becomes a standard systemic-risk indicator next to the VIX: the crowd's belief in how connected the world is.

**Scores.** **Innovation 6.** DFS stacks and same-game parlays are close neighbours. Pari-mutuel joint cells, resolution floors, mark-to-jump scoring and a correlation matrix as the output are a new category. **Feasibility 6.** The engine is ordinary. Feed licensing, a weekly cadence that fights roguelite appetite, and the state sweepstakes patchwork are real drags.

### Turn 6 — Builder
I'm scoring innovation 5, not 6. Racetracks have run pari-mutuel pools on joint outcomes for decades in exactas and trifectas, and DFS stacking already turned correlation into a game. The new parts are applying that across domains, marking scores to the price jump at each resolution, and treating the correlation matrix as the product. That's bold, but not 6. Feasibility 6 stands: the engine is ordinary, and the real risk is the skill-persistence test. If last month's top decile doesn't beat the field this month, no one will license a matrix built from luck.

# Idea 13 — Canary Ladder
> Seed: 13. [wild] **Post-quantum cryptography × prediction markets × insurance.** Hedging Q-Day. Who underwrites the end of ECC?

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Canary Ladder

**Hook:** A parametric insurance market whose trigger is a proof of the peril itself. Cover pays out when someone forges a signature that only a cryptographically relevant quantum computer could produce.

**The problem.** Parametric cover needs an objective trigger. Q-Day has none: lab press releases hype, and a state that breaks ECC hides it. Cryptography, unlike a hurricane index, can make the event **self-certifying**.

**Core loop.** The verb is **climb**.
1. **The ladder.** An MPC ceremony generates elliptic-curve keys on curves from 160 to 256 bits. Private keys are destroyed; recovering one needs every share. The top rung is a real secp256k1 key. Each rung guards a bounty in a contract that pays whoever signs their own address with that rung's key. Rungs start past the public classical record (about 130-bit kangaroo solves), so a fall means Shor or a classical breakthrough. Either way, ECC is weaker than priced.
2. **The curve.** Contracts like "rung 224 falls before 2030" form a **Q-Day term structure**: a hazard curve priced by money, not keynotes.
3. **The cover.** Exchanges, custodians and exposed-pubkey holders buy protection paying in tranches as rungs fall. Quantum skeptics underwrite it the way cat-bond investors underwrite hurricanes, earning premium until the canary dies.
4. **The discount.** Premiums fall when exposure verifiably migrates to ML-DSA or BIP-360 outputs, so insurance becomes the migration incentive.

**Why the collision works.** Markets need resolution, insurance a trigger, crypto a canary. One mechanism serves all three. The bounty also pays an attacker to *reveal* capability rather than hoard it, at least up to the bounty's size.

**Why now.** A EUROCRYPT 2026 estimate puts ECC-256 at about 1,098 logical qubits. Helios has about 48 logical qubits at 99.9995%. Google set a 2029 PQC deadline, and BIP-360 is still a draft (brief §2, §6, #5).

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** The ladder already exists by accident. The 2015 Bitcoin puzzle transaction funded keys of increasing size, and kangaroo solvers have taken the exposed-pubkey rungs up to 130 bits (2024). Certicom's ECC challenges have stood since 1997. Project Eleven's Q-Day Prize offered 1 BTC for a Shor break of a toy key. Metaculus and Manifold list unfunded Q-Day questions. Nexus Mutual sells crypto cover with discretionary claims, and Lloyd's now requires exclusions for state-backed cyber attacks, so nobody underwrites this peril.

**Hardest problem: the collateral dies with the canary.** If cover is paid in USDC or ETH, the payout asset, the chain's validator signatures and the contract's admin keys all rest on the curves that just fell. The insurer pays in money that is crashing because of the insured event. That's wrong-way risk at its purest. The top rung also can't compete: whoever breaks secp256k1 takes Satoshi's roughly 1M exposed-pubkey BTC instead of the bounty. Bitcoin is already the 256-bit canary.

**MVP: a ladder you can price this year.** Add **calibration rungs** at 135, 140, 145 and 150 bits, where GPU cost is known (thousands of GPU-years at 140 bits). Those rungs fall to money, so near-dated contracts resolve now and fit the classical cost curve. The quantum signal is a rung falling *ahead of* that curve, not a rung falling. Generate keys by distributed key generation, where one honest participant suffices, and accept claims only with hash-based (SLH-DSA) signatures behind a commit-reveal.

**Twist: a cat bond with a cryptographic trigger.** Keep the trigger on-chain and the money off the curve. A Bermuda SPV holds T-bills in trust, exactly as every cat bond does, and pays tranches when rungs fall. Insurance-linked-securities funds want perils uncorrelated with hurricanes, and Q-Day is. The skeptics already have a desk.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
Cat bond and calibration rungs both taken; neither shrinks the idea. The rungs supply the real instrument: **the quantum signal is a rung falling ahead of the classical cost curve.** Time-to-fall against GPU-years becomes a public ruler of cryptographic relevance. IBM's Quantum Advantage Tracker measures advantage on benchmarks nobody steals with. This one measures it where the money is.

**Wrong-way risk, finished.** The SPV moves the money off the curve, but the *trigger* still sits on a chain whose signatures may be failing. Remove the chain from the trigger. In a cat bond, a calculation agent declares the event (PCS or Verisk for hurricanes). Here the trust deed names a **verification function**: a valid signature on the claimant's message under rung key Pₖ, committed with SLH-DSA. Anyone can check it offline. This would be the first cat bond whose calculation agent is a theorem.

**Satoshi's problem becomes the feature.** Builder is right that no bounty competes with 1M BTC, and a state attacker never claims anything. So stop relying on claims. The unseeded domain is **deception security**: honeypots and Thinkst-style canary tokens.
- **Prey that sings.** Seed thousands of decoy wallets with exposed pubkeys, made by DKG to look like ordinary address reuse, across balance bands. A quiet harvester sweeping exposed coins will sweep decoys too. A decoy moving proves capability that was never meant to be revealed.
- **Harvest-now-decrypt-later tripwires.** Publish ECDH-encrypted blobs along high-value routes, each holding a live credential to a monitored lure. Decrypt one and use it, and the wire trips.

**Second-order effect.** The ladder measures *public* capability and the decoys measure *covert* capability. The spread between the two curves is the market's price of a hidden quantum computer: a number that intelligence agencies guard today, published as a bond spread.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**Kills and fixes.**
- *Small decoys as early warning.* Early quantum machines take hours per key, so harvesters hit the biggest exposed balances first. Small decoys trip late; big ones cost real capital. Bitcoin already holds natural decoys, about 1M BTC of dormant Satoshi-era P2PK outputs; index their movement for free. Our decoys keep one job: **the cheapest balance band swept reveals the attacker's cost per key.**
- *HNDL tripwires.* A state decrypting archives in 2035 won't log into a lure. Moonshot only.
- *Custom curves.* Use secp256k1 with **range-restricted keys**, like the 2015 puzzle. Ekerå–Håstad short-log Shor scales with key length, so one rung is both a GPU target and a quantum target, and it can hold a native bounty.

**Moral hazard.** Whoever owns a CRQC knows the trigger first and buys cover. Sell protection only to buyers with verified exposure. The prediction market stays open to informed traders; that leak is the signal. Ceremony fraud also fakes a trigger. Put **buyers and underwriters both in the DKG**, so faking it requires collusion across opposed sides.

**Business model.** Structuring fees from custodian and exchange sponsors, ILS funds as investors, and the rung and dormant-coin series licensed as a **Q-Day index**. Pitch Kalshi on listing rung contracts with our verifier as the resolution source.

**Stack.** DKG for range-restricted keys, an SLH-DSA claim protocol, an offline verifier in two languages, a Bermuda special-purpose insurer selling under Rule 144A.

**Safety.** A false trigger could crash crypto markets, so run two independent ladders and require both.

**90 days.** Weeks 1–4: ceremony, funded rungs at 135–160 bits. Weeks 5–8: verifier, dormant-coin index, trust deed with Bermuda counsel. Weeks 9–12: term sheet for a private $10–25M bond.
**Kill metrics:** no ILS fund will price a tranche, or no calibration rung draws a credible solver.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
All three kills accepted. Range-restricted keys are the thread's best fix: one rung is both GPU and Shor target. Satoshi's coins beat any decoys we could fund. One pushback: "both ladders must trip" lets whoever corrupts one ceremony suppress a real trigger. Use three ladders; trigger on two.

**Final form.** Canary Ladder is a cat bond with a cryptographic trigger.
- **Ladder.** Three DKG ceremonies, buyers and underwriters in each, produce range-restricted secp256k1 keys from 135 to 256 effective bits, each with a native bounty.
- **Trigger.** A trust deed names an offline verifier as calculation agent: an SLH-DSA-committed signature under a rung key, on two of three ladders.
- **Collateral.** A Bermuda SPV holds T-bills off the curve and pays tranches as rungs fall.
- **Q-Day index.** Rung fall times against the GPU-cost curve, dormant P2PK movement, and the cheapest decoy band swept. Kalshi lists rung contracts on our verifier.
- **Migration discount.** Cover is sold only to buyers with verified exposure, and premiums fall as exposure migrates to post-quantum outputs.

**Moonshot (2036): a futures market in mathematical hardness.** Q-Day is one peril among many. SIKE, a NIST round-4 candidate, fell to a classical laptop attack in 2022; lattices could too. Every deployed assumption, including ML-KEM, ML-DSA, SLH-DSA and RSA, gets a standing ladder and a hazard curve. NIST and central banks set deprecation dates from market-priced hardness, not committee calendars. Assumption cover becomes as routine as hurricane cover, and the market pays people to stop trusting maths early.

**Scores.** **Innovation 8.** Cryptographic hardness becomes an insurable peril with a self-certifying trigger: a new institution whose oracle is a theorem. **Feasibility 4.** Ladder, verifier and index ship in 90 days. A peril with no loss history and untested ILS appetite is where it stalls.

### Turn 6 — Builder
Two-of-three is right. I'd been guarding against false triggers and left the suppression attack open. Innovation 8 stands: the Bitcoin puzzle and cat bonds already exist, but making cryptographic hardness an insured peril, with a theorem acting as calculation agent, is a new institution, and the strange core survived every fix. Feasibility 4 also stands. The ladder, verifier and index can ship by Christmas, while a peril with no loss history may never clear an ILS committee. If the bond stalls, the Q-Day index is still worth building on its own.
