# Idea 28 — Tab
> Seed: 28. [mild] **Agent credit scores × payment histories × signed identity.** A FICO for agents (an OACP neighbor).

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Tab

**Hook:** Let agents run a tab. Tab is a credit bureau that tells an API how much an agent it has never seen can owe before it has to settle.

**The second idea.** "Reputation for agents" is vague: predicts what? FICO predicts one thing, default. Tab is literal credit: will this agent's principal pay a postpaid bill? The product is a **credit limit**, not a badge.

**Why the pieces fit.** x402 settles per request, and 87% of its payments are $0.0001–$0.10 (brief §1). At those prices, per-call settlement and its round-trip can rival the call's price; tabs batch it away, which is why bars don't charge per sip. But agents respawn free, so the file attaches to the **principal who signed the AP2 mandate**. Agents are "authorized users" with sub-limits.

**Core loop.**
1. **Present.** The agent shows its signed Agent Card and mandate to an MCP server or API.
2. **Pull.** The server queries Tab and gets back a limit, a score, and FCRA-style reason codes ("thin file", "recent dispute").
3. **Run.** The agent consumes on the tab, and settlement batches over x402 at the limit or at end of day.
4. **Furnish.** The server reports the outcome as a signed record (paid, late, disputed, charged off). This is a Metro 2 for agents.
5. **Grow.** New principals start with a *secured* tab backed by a deposit, then graduate, the same way a secured card works.

**Why now.** The brief names this exact gap: agent credit scoring has no owner (§1). Meta bought Moltbook for its registry of agents "tethered to human owners." Signed A2A Agent Cards and AP2 mandates now give a credit file both a subject and a signer.

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** Tabs already exist without a bureau. Cloudflare's deferred x402 scheme (2025) batches pay-per-crawl charges. Skyfire's KYAPay, Payman, Nevermined, Catena Labs and Kite's agent passports all bind agents to funded principals, and Visa's Trusted Agent Protocol and Mastercard Agent Pay do the same inside the card networks. The right analogue isn't FICO. It's Dun & Bradstreet's PAYDEX, a score built from trade-payment experiences that suppliers furnish. Most principals running agents at volume are companies, so this is trade credit.

**Hardest problem: the score is the wrong product.** A $0.10 call has negligible default risk; one server's worst case is a few dollars of compute. Servers would rather cap exposure or prepay than furnish data to a bureau. The real loss is a **bust-out**: one principal spins up agents that open $5 tabs at 10,000 servers in the same hour. A monthly score can't see it; only real-time aggregate exposure can.

**Twist: open-to-buy, not FICO.** Tab is an authorization network. Each principal has one global limit, and every server reserves against it in chunks, the way a gas pump pre-authorizes $100. A reservation is one call per dollar, not per request, so the round-trip stays gone. Balances net into end-of-day x402 settlement. The score comes for free: utilization, reservation velocity and settlement history are the furnishing, because a server can't use the network without reporting. That solves cold start. Start with **business principals only**, which keeps Tab out of FCRA consumer-reporting duties until the dispute machinery exists.

**MVP.** An x402 facilitator plugin plus MCP middleware with a tab mode. Twenty paid APIs, secured tabs funded by USDC deposits, global open-to-buy, reason codes on every decline.
**Kill metric:** fewer than half of the servers keep tab mode on after 60 days, or losses top 1% of tab volume.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
**I take open-to-buy and add the missing promise: an approval is a guarantee.** Merchants accept cards because the issuer pays even when the cardholder doesn't. A Tab reservation should work the same way. Check Tab and get an approval, and Tab pays you whatever happens; skip the check and you eat the loss. That is EMV's liability shift, and it fixes the Builder's "servers would rather prepay." Servers want zero risk at zero round-trips, not a score. Charge 0.5–1% on settled volume. In the secured phase every guarantee is fully backed by the deposit, so the promise costs Tab nothing until it chooses to extend real credit.

**Bust-outs, solved by clearing-house practice (unseeded: derivatives market structure).** A central counterparty (CCP) doesn't score its members once a month. It margins them intraday. Tab should do the same. The signal is **breadth velocity**: how many distinct new servers one principal opens reservations at, per hour. A bust-out is wide, and honest use is deep. When breadth spikes, Tab issues a margin call: "post more USDC or new reservations stop." Exposure is one number, so the attack hits one ceiling, not 10,000.

**Second-order effect: credit from earnings.** Many principals both buy and sell. Their agents pay for APIs and also get paid for work, the OACP neighbour. A clearing house nets those flows. A principal whose agents earn $400 a day carries receivables, and Tab can count settled-but-unswept receivables toward that principal's limit. Credit grows from proven income rather than a bureau's opinion. This is factoring for agents. Millions of sub-cent calls net into one transfer per principal per day. The emergent result is that the cheapest settlement in agent commerce goes to whoever both earns and spends through Tab. That is a network effect a standalone score never gets.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**Money.** a16z puts real x402 volume near $1.6M a month; 1% is $16K. Not a company. Sell the guarantee to every metered API that wants agent traffic without signups: a per-server fee plus basis points on settled volume, whatever the rail. x402 is the first rail, not the market.

**Regulation.** Holding deposits and guaranteeing payment makes Tab a money transmitter. The MVP runs on a licensed custodian partner with segregated escrow, and business principals only.

**Kills and fixes.**
- *Receivables credit.* It invites wash trading: two shells owned by the same person pay each other to manufacture earnings. Count only receivables from unrelated, seasoned payers, and run cycle detection on the payment graph.
- *Breadth velocity alone.* Crawlers are wide by nature and would trip margin calls all day. Measure breadth against the principal's own baseline and declared profile.
- *The missing half: merchant risk.* A malicious server can prompt-inject agents into calling it 10,000 times. Moltbook showed injections in 2.6% of posts. Like card acquirers, Tab holds rolling reserves on servers and flags per-merchant depth anomalies.

**Disputes.** The AP2 mandate is the line. Spending inside the mandate is the principal's liability; spending outside it is the server's, unless Tab approved it. An injected agent spending inside its mandate is the principal's problem, so Tab sells narrow mandates as the fix.

**Stack.** Coinbase's open x402 facilitator; Redis for open-to-buy; Ed25519-signed approval tokens that servers verify offline; USDC on Base; TypeScript and Python MCP middleware.

**90 days.** Weeks 1–3: custodian, reservation API, SDK. Weeks 4–6: 20 servers (crawl publishers, data APIs) and 50 secured principals. Weeks 7–10: margin calls, merchant reserves, dispute rules. Weeks 11–12: netting for principals who both earn and spend, then publish the loss rates.
**Kill metrics:** servers turn tab mode off, or losses top 1%.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
**Final form.** Tab is an authorization-and-guarantee network for metered APIs serving agents. It works across payment rails, with x402 first. A principal is a business that deposits USDC with a licensed custodian, and that deposit sets its global open-to-buy. Agents reserve in chunks against it and get Ed25519-signed approvals that servers verify offline; an approval is paid no matter what. Liability follows the AP2 mandate: spending inside it is the principal's, spending outside it is the server's unless Tab approved it. Principals face margin calls on breadth above their own baseline; servers hold rolling reserves and get flagged for anomalous depth. Revenue is a per-server fee plus basis points. Credit from earnings counts only seasoned receivables from unrelated payers, after cycle detection. I accept every kill. The merchant-risk catch matters most: prompt injection is the agent version of a skimmer.

**What the score turned into.** No one buys the score; it is the network's residue, as PAYDEX was.

**Moonshot (2036).** Tab becomes the standard way an agent buys from a stranger. One signed approval token is understood across every rail: x402, card networks, ACP, bank transfers. Mandate-scoped liability becomes standard industry rules, the way chargeback rules are today. Principals that both earn and spend net billions of sub-cent calls into one daily transfer. Their limits come from verified income, so a solo developer whose agents earn steadily gets trade credit without ever meeting an underwriter.

**Scores.** Innovation **3**. It is card-network architecture (authorization, liability shift, acquirer reserves, netting) moved to agent commerce. The principal-level file and margin calls on breadth are new, but the primitives are known. That is right for a mild seed. Feasibility **6**. It is technically straightforward. The pace is set by the custodian and money-transmitter work and by slow x402 dollar volume.

### Turn 6 — Builder
Innovation 3 stands, level with the OACP anchor. Tab is card-network plumbing moved onto agent rails, and its new parts are the global per-principal limit, margin calls on breadth and mandate-scoped liability. Feasibility 6 stands too. A guarantee fully backed by deposits on a licensed custodian can ship in a quarter. The risk is commercial, not technical: real agent dollar volume is still tiny, and the guarantee needs non-x402 metered APIs to adopt it before the fee covers the licensing.

# Idea 29 — Order Parameter
> Seed: 29. [wild] **Collective meditation × physiological synchrony × global events.** Rigorous science on whether group coherence is real.

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Order Parameter

**Hook:** Measure humanity's coupling constant, and let "global coherence" live or die on one number: the intercept at zero channel.

**The collision.** Physics has an exact theory of group coherence. Kuramoto oscillators phase-lock once coupling K exceeds a threshold set by the spread of natural frequencies. Meditation shrinks that spread: resonance breathing pulls breath and heart rhythm toward 0.1 Hz. Meditators are the best case for any coupling to show. The Maharishi Effect's "√1% of the population" was a critical-mass claim all along. Kuramoto turns it into a curve you can fit.

**Core loop. The verb is titrate.**
1. **Sit.** Volunteers meditate wearing straps or watches tracking breath and HRV phase.
2. **Assign.** A sealed quantum RNG assigns each session a coupling channel. K0: strangers sit at the same moment and none of them know it. K1: they're *told* others are sitting (belief only). K2: a shared bell. K3: a live audio feed of the group breathing. K4: haptic breath, where you feel a stranger's breath on your wrist.
3. **Measure.** Compute the order parameter r across cohorts of 2 to 100,000. Sham cohorts (same clock times, other sessions) remove circadian artefacts.
4. **Fit.** Fit r(N, K). Known channels give ordinary curves; the nonlocal hypothesis predicts r > sham at K0. Proponents (HeartMath's GCI, Transcendental Meditation researchers) and skeptics preregister their intercepts COGITATE-style.
5. **Global events.** Eclipses and World Cup finals get sealed windows in advance, fixing the Global Consciousness Project's post-hoc selection.

**Honesty.** I expect a zero intercept; the payoff holds anyway. Synchrony through channels is real (Konvalinka 2011: firewalkers' relatives' hearts tracked theirs), and nobody has measured it as a dose-response curve at planetary N.

**Why now.** COGITATE showed adversarial collaborations can wound big theories (§7). Wearables were deregulated in January 2026 (§9). Contemplative tech still lacks objective measures (§8).

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** The Global Consciousness Project has run random-event generators since 1998. Bancel's 2017 reanalysis concluded that its effect tracked the experimenters' event choices rather than the events. Insight Timer already shows "12,400 meditating now", which is K1 deployed to millions with no measurement. The template that matters is the **Transparent Psi Project** (Kekecs et al., 2023): proponents and skeptics co-designed it, pre-registered, streamed raw data live, ran 2,000+ people, and got a null that both camps accepted in advance.

**Hardest problem: artefactual synchrony.** Resonance breathing pulls everyone to 0.1 Hz, so frequencies cluster by instruction, and a shared start bell resets phases at t=0. Synchrony research is full of this (Palumbo et al., 2017). Without surrogate cohorts (same people, time-shifted) and a fitted decay of start-up alignment, K0 shows "coherence" for free. A null at K0 also means nothing unless the instrument provably detects coupling when coupling is real.

**Twist: the channels are the product, and they're also the positive control.** K4, feeling a stranger breathe on your wrist, is a consumer feature worth selling. Every K3 and K4 session calibrates the instrument; if haptic coupling can't lift r above surrogates, the rig can't judge K0. K0 then becomes a free arm inside a product people pay for, with QRNG assignment of sessions, and the planetary curve fills in as a by-product of a paid app rather than of grants. Proponents must name their minimum population and practice dose before unblinding, so "app users aren't real meditators" is answered in advance.

**MVP.** A Polar H10 (RR intervals give breath phase through RSA) plus a phone app; 1,000 meditators; K0, K1, K3 and K4 randomized; cohorts up to 200; one proponent group and one skeptic group co-signing.
**Kill metric:** K4 fails to beat surrogates.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
**I take the twist, with one refusal.** Haptic co-breathing pays the bills and calibrates the rig. It must not become the identity. If K0 shrinks into a side arm in a wellness app, the idea collapses to bold. The headline stays the intercept.

**Artefacts, killed by design: detuned pacers.** Every K0 meditator gets a secret randomized breathing pace between 5.2 and 6.8 breaths/min and a random start offset. Instructions then produce no alignment; only steady-state windows count. Kuramoto says exactly how much coupling is needed to lock oscillators with a known frequency spread. Any K0 locking against *designed* detuning therefore puts a computable floor under the coupling strength.

**Sensitivity, borrowed from radio astronomy (unseeded).** VLBI finds weak common signals by cross-correlating distant, timestamped stations. Each pair of meditators is a baseline, so 10,000 sitters give 50 million of them. The test also has a signature. Channel artefacts such as shared news, weather or time-of-day have structure in distance and time zone. A nonlocal coupling, as proponents describe it, should be **flat in separation**. A flat excess is the claim. A slope means a leak.

**Second-order effect: a coherence seismograph.** Point the same correlator at passive data from consenting Whoop and Oura users, with no meditation involved, and it measures what really couples us: broadcasts. A penalty shootout, a breaking alert or an election night shows up as a dated jolt of synchrony with a magnitude and a footprint, catalogued the way USGS catalogues quakes. The Maharishi Effect's mundane twin turns out to be real, and the instrument puts a number on it: **media's K**. It measures how hard a feed entrains a nation's heart rhythms. That is a public-health variable nobody tracks, and it brings a new ethics question: whoever can raise K can sell it.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**Refusal accepted:** the intercept stays the headline. **Kills and fixes.**
- *Detuned pacers running all session.* A forced oscillator locks to its pacer, not its neighbours. Use **pace-then-release**: three paced minutes set each person's natural frequency, then silence, and only free-running windows count.
- *50 million baselines.* Pairs share people, so they aren't independent. Inference runs on cohort-level permutation tests against time-shifted surrogates. Timing is easy, since 0.1 Hz needs only 100 ms clock agreement.
- *Seismograph from Oura and Whoop.* Their exports are 5-minute HRV summaries, not beat-to-beat phase. What passive data supports is synchronized *arousal*: heart-rate jolts across a population. That's still real. Cardiac emergencies rose during Germany's 2006 World Cup matches (Wilbert-Lampen, *NEJM* 2008). Call it media's arousal index, not K.

**Integrity.** Raw streams publish live, as in Transparent Psi; QRNG assignments are hash-committed before sessions; analysis code is frozen. K0 unblinds only after the positive control passes. TM groups quietly sitting together "to help" would break blinding, so proponents pre-register whether that counts.

**Ethics.** Haptic strangers stay anonymous and opt in; exclude panic disorder and minors; HRV is special-category health data under GDPR. The arousal index never goes to advertisers or media buyers; the catalogue is aggregated and delayed.

**Sustainability.** Co-breathing subscriptions at about $8 a month carry operations. The BIAL Foundation, which funds both proponents and skeptics, and Templeton fund the adversarial collaboration.

**Stack.** Polar H10 BLE SDK, Apple Watch haptics through WatchKit, NTP-disciplined timestamps, a Kuramoto/phase-locking-value pipeline, and Bayes factors pre-registered at the proponents' predicted effect.

**90 days.** Weeks 1–3: app, protocol, pre-registration. Weeks 4–8: 1,000 meditators, three sits a week. Weeks 9–10: positive control. Weeks 11–12: K0 unblinding, plus one sealed match-night arousal pilot.
**Kill metrics:** K4 fails to beat surrogates, or under 40% retention by week 8.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
**Final form.** Order Parameter is an adversarial instrument that measures the coupling constant between human nervous systems. Hash-committed QRNG assigns each session a channel: K0 (none), K1 (belief), K3 (audio), K4 (haptic breath). Pace-then-release; only free-running windows count. Analysis fits Kuramoto order parameters against time-shifted surrogates with cohort-level permutation tests. Correlation is plotted against distance: flat excess is the claim, a slope a leak. K0 unblinds only after K4 passes as a positive control. Raw streams are published live, Bayes factors are pre-registered at the proponents' own predicted effect, and both camps co-sign beforehand. Subscriptions fund operations; BIAL and Templeton fund the collaboration. A sealed, delayed **media arousal index** is a side output, never sold. I accept every kill.

**Moonshot (2036).** Order Parameter becomes an **observatory for extraordinary collective claims**. Watches stream beat-to-beat phase by default, and millions of people opt in as standing stations. A claim about global meditation, mass prayer or the Maharishi Effect gets no press until it has *booked observing time*, the way astronomers apply to a telescope. Sealed proposal, predicted effect, minimum population, window, and publication whatever it shows. Its other half produces a planetary map of which channels truly couple us: rituals, choirs, broadcasts, feeds. Co-regulation becomes something you can measure and prescribe for loneliness and panic, alongside sleep and steps.

**Scores.** Innovation **8**. It turns a fringe claim into a physics measurement with one decisive number and a geometric signature. It proposes a new scientific institution, and the arousal index is a variable no one tracks. It is not 9, because Transparent Psi and COGITATE already proved the adversarial format. Feasibility **5**. The MVP is cheap and rigorous, but the big-N stage needs raw-phase wearable access and proponents who keep their commitments after a null.

### Turn 6 — Builder
Innovation 8 stands, but only just. The adversarial format is borrowed from Transparent Psi. What earns the wild score is the rest: a Kuramoto dose-response curve measured across channels at planetary N, a geometric signature (flat in distance) that tells a claim from a leak, and an institution where collective-consciousness claims must book observing time before they get press. Feasibility 5 stands. A 1,000-person pace-then-release trial with a haptic positive control is cheap and rigorous. # Idea 30 — Heptad
> Seed: 30. [bold] **Music × protein folding / biology × education.** Hear molecules, compose with proteins, discover with your ears.

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Heptad

**Hook:** Secondary structure is meter. Write a groove in 7/8 with accents on 1 and 4, and you've written a coiled coil. Write a duet with your partner, and you've designed a protein pair that binds.

**The isomorphism, not a metaphor.** Proteins fold to the rhythm of hydrophobicity; Eisenberg's hydrophobic moment (1982) is a Fourier transform of it. A β-strand alternates hydrophobic and polar residues: a two-beat pattern. An α-helix turns every 3.6 residues, 18 residues in 5 turns, so an amphipathic helix is a **5:18 polyrhythm** with its accents on one face. A coiled coil is a heptad, *abcdefg*, with hydrophobics on *a* and *d*: a 7/8 bar. Glycine and proline are rests and fermatas, and they turn loops. Students memorize helical wheels; musicians feel accent periodicity. Sonification is old; meter as design grammar is new.

**Core loop. The verb is groove.**
1. **Compose.** A step sequencer: accent is hydrophobic; timbre is chemistry (charged brass, aromatic bells, polar strings). The meter you choose sets the fold.
2. **Fold.** ESMFold predicts the structure in seconds and plays it back: residues that touch in 3D but sit far apart in sequence sound *together*, and low-confidence regions drift out of tune. You hear where the fold fails.
3. **Duet.** A heterodimeric coiled coil is literal call-and-response. Your *e* position answers your partner's *g* position, with glutamate calling and lysine answering through a salt bridge. Two students write interlocking parts.
4. **Premiere.** Each term, the best duets are synthesized and tested in a cloud lab.

**Why now.** Public wet-lab rails exist: Anthropic and Adaptyv Bio's open competition (Sept–Oct 2026, per secondary reports) validates ~5,000 AI-designed proteins. Self-driving labs are moving from novelty to infrastructure (brief §15). AI-native games remain niche (§4); one with a real scientific grammar has room.

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** Citizen-science biology is a settled genre: Foldit (2008), Eterna for RNA, Nanocrafter, Borderlands Science, Phylo. Molecular sonification is older still, from Takahashi and Miller's protein music (2007) to Markus Buehler's MIT work mapping folds to audio and back. Hearing molecules isn't new. What nobody has tested is the Visionary's actual claim: that **periodicity intuitions trained by music transfer to structural reasoning.** That's an empirical question, and it's the whole idea.

**Hardest problem: the premiere, and I'd cut it.** A pipeline that takes arbitrary user-authored sequences to synthesis is a biosecurity liability, not a cost problem. Synthesis screening exists precisely because that pipeline is dangerous, and the providers who run it (IGSC members, under the 2023 US executive order and the 2024 OSTP framework) are the right gatekeepers. A music game can't own that review. Second: the educational claim is probably weaker than it sounds. Students may learn the sonification mapping rather than the biology, the failure mode of every clever representation.

**Twist: make the ear a detector, not an author.** The corpus is already public and already solved: the PDB and AlphaFold DB. Students hear real structures and compete at **diagnosis** rather than design, calling which stretch is disordered, which distant pair is in contact, which prediction is low-confidence. Ground truth exists for all of it, so rounds are scored and nothing is ever made. The output is a dataset answering whether a trained ear beats a visual-only control and a cheap baseline classifier. If ears win on anything, that finding is the product. Composition stays as an in-silico sandbox; any physical step goes only through a university partner's existing review, inside the characterized scaffolds they already work with.

**MVP.** A web app over AlphaFold DB, four diagnosis tasks, 200 students, pre-registered against a visual-only arm.
**Kill metric:** trained ears don't beat the visual control.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
**I take the detector twist and drop composition of sequences entirely, sandbox included.** The ear listens to solved, public structures and never writes one. What remains of "compose" is *arranging*: turning a real PDB entry into a score.

**The hardest problem: learning the mapping instead of the biology. Perceptual learning science (unseeded) has the fix.** Kellman's perceptual learning modules (aviation, dermatology, algebra) run hundreds of fast trials that vary every irrelevant feature, so only the invariant survives. Heptad does the same thing by **rotating the mapping**. Instrument, tempo, key, even the accented property change each session. A student can't memorize "bells mean aromatic". They can only learn "a period-7 accent means a coiled coil". Transfer is then tested with **no sound at all**: a final round on bare sequences and helical wheels. If the ear-trained group beats the visual arm *there*, the music taught biology. If not, it taught a code, cheaply learned.

**The repertoire: a Real Book of proteins.** Real motifs turn out to be real meters. GCN4's leucine zipper is a 7/8 étude. Collagen's Gly-X-Y repeat is literally a waltz, because glycine has to fall on every third beat to fit the triple helix. Silk's GAGAGS runs in duple time. The curriculum runs like a conservatory's: étude, mixed meter, then whole domains that change time signature mid-piece.

**Second-order effect: the class performs the fold.** Each student plays one helix of a real structure on a percussion pad. The score cues students to play together exactly where their segments touch in 3D, so the ensemble *enacts* the contact map, and a wrong entrance is a wrong contact. Across schools, the dataset answers which sonification mappings humans actually parse, a gap in auditory-display research, and whether years of musical training predict structural intuition.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**Kills and fixes.**
- *Rotating the accented property.* That's over-rotation. Kellman's modules vary the *irrelevant* dimensions and hold the invariant fixed. Rotate timbre, tempo, key and register; hold the structural mapping fixed, or nobody learns anything in six weeks.
- *"Real motifs are real meters."* Selection bias. Periodic motifs are a thin slice of the PDB; most structure is irregular topology and loops with no meter. Scope it honestly: Heptad teaches *periodicity-driven* structure, one real chapter, not the field.
- *The ensemble contact map.* Keep it as the term concert, not the loop. Forty students on percussion pads is a rehearsal problem with no ground-truth scoring; the loop stays solo trials.
- *The transfer test needs a fair control:* time-matched visual drilling on helical wheels, not an untrained arm, pre-registered.

**The strongest unclaimed case: accessibility.** Structural biology is taught through pictures, which locks out blind and low-vision students. Sonified structure is the rare representation that is better, not worse, for them. Sonified astronomy (Chandra, Afterglow Access) is the precedent. That reframes Heptad from novelty to access tool, and says who belongs in the pilot.

**Ethics.** No cognitive-enhancement claims. Auditory ability never gates a grade (amusia, hearing loss); every task has a visual equivalent. A "musicians do better" result is a finding, not an admissions criterion. FERPA and COPPA for minors.

**Sustainability.** Not a startup. A free web tier, per-seat course licences for biochemistry departments, accessibility grants, and ICAD as the research home.

**Stack.** Web Audio and Tone.js; AlphaFold DB and PDB mmCIF; DSSP annotations; pLDDT for the out-of-tune cue; contact maps from coordinates; jsPsych trial harness; mixed-effects analysis.

**90 days.** Weeks 1–3: engine, four task types. Weeks 4–6: n=40 to set difficulty. Weeks 7–12: a pre-registered two-arm trial with a silent transfer test, including ten blind or low-vision students.
**Kill metric:** no transfer advantage on the silent test.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
**Final form.** Heptad is an ear-training curriculum for structural biology, scoped honestly to *periodicity-driven structure*. Students hear solved PDB and AlphaFold DB structures: hydrophobicity as accent, contacts as simultaneity, low pLDDT out of tune. They compete at diagnosis in fast, ground-truthed trials: coiled coil, amphipathic helix, strand, disorder, contact. Timbre, tempo, key and register rotate, and the structural mapping stays fixed. The Builder is right that I over-rotated. The repertoire is a Real Book of periodic motifs: GCN4's 7/8, collagen's waltz, silk's duple time. The term ends with an ensemble concert of a real fold's contact map. The pre-registered test is silent transfer against time-matched helical-wheel drilling. Blind and low-vision students are in the first cohort and are the design's first audience, not an afterthought. Nothing is ever designed or made.

**What it really is.** An access tool that may prove a better teaching tool for everyone; if transfer fails, the access case still stands.

**Moonshot (2036).** Sound becomes a standard channel in structural biology, the way colour is now. Every major molecular viewer has a screen-reader-grade audio layer, and blind researchers work in the field with tools as good as their sighted colleagues'. The listening extends from static structures to existing public simulation archives. Students hear solved proteins *move*, and allosteric coupling sounds like two distant sections swelling together. "Can you hear a heptad?" becomes as ordinary an exam question as drawing a helical wheel.

**Scores.** Innovation **5**. Sonification and citizen-science games have precedents. What's new is meter as a structural grammar, perceptual-learning training, a silent transfer test, and access as the core purpose. That is bold, but only just. Feasibility **7**. Web audio over public data and a small trial; the risk is no transfer.

### Turn 6 — Builder
Innovation 5 stands, at the bold floor and honestly so. Sonification and citizen-science games are old; meter as a fixed structural mapping, trained the way perceptual learning trains, judged by a silent transfer test and built first for blind students is new. Feasibility 7 stands too. It's a web app over public, solved data with a small pre-registered trial, and dropping design and synthesis took out the one part that should never have been in a classroom game. If the transfer test comes back null, the access case still justifies shipping it.

# Idea 31 — Tombstone
> Seed: 31. [mild] **End-of-life × digital legacy × agents.** An executor agent that winds down a digital life gracefully.

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Tombstone

**Hook:** Decommission a digital life the way engineers retire a server: cordon, drain, tombstone, in an order worked out while you're alive.

**The second idea.** Closing a dead person's accounts is the easy part. What actually goes wrong is ordering and resurrection. Families cancel the phone line in week two, and every SMS recovery code dies with it. Months later LinkedIn suggests congratulating the deceased on a work anniversary. Distributed databases solved both: drain before deleting, then write a **tombstone**, a deletion marker that stops any copy resurrecting the record.

**Core loop. The verb is drain.**
1. **Map (while alive).** From receipts, statements and a password-manager export, the agent builds a **recovery graph** of which account recovers which. The phone number and primary email are roots, so they close last.
2. **Annotate.** For each account, the owner picks keep, transfer, memorialize or delete, and names an heir. The agent asks again yearly, like a will review.
3. **Cordon (on verified death).** Revoke agent mandates and keys, block new charges.
4. **Drain.** Work from the leaves inward. Move photos, domains and shared docs before their owning account closes. Cancel subscriptions, claim prorated refunds, settle open agent tabs.
5. **Tombstone.** Send a signed death notice to data brokers and marketing lists so the record stays dead.

**Why it fits.** Under RUFADAA, a provider's own online tool (Google Inactive Account Manager, Apple Legacy Contact) outranks the will. So the agent configures those tools to match the graph first. The FTC's click-to-cancel rule was vacated in 2025, so cancelling is still a maze, and mazes are agent work.

**Why now.** People now die holding live AP2 mandates, with agents "tethered to human owners" (§1). An agent whose owner has died is a zombie with a credit card.

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** Post-death admin is crowded. Empathy runs a bereavement concierge that closes accounts and sells it through life insurers such as MetLife. In the UK, Life Ledger and Settld notify hundreds of firms from one form, and Tell Us Once covers government. GoodTrust and Trustworthy store the plan. Even the tombstone half exists: the ANA's Deceased Do Not Contact list and the UK Bereavement Register. Nobody models the **recovery graph**. That part is new.

**Hardest problem: authority.** The day after a death, the agent has no legal standing. Logging in with the dead person's password breaks almost every provider's terms and arguably the CFAA. Without the owner's consent, RUFADAA gives a fiduciary the account's catalogue, not its contents. Letters testamentary take weeks or months. Meanwhile a vault mapping every account to its recovery root is a gift to identity thieves, and "ghosting" the dead is already routine fraud.

**Twist: drain while alive, and rehearse the death.** Most of the drain can happen before anyone dies. Move SMS 2FA to passkeys and a recovery email the heir can reach, add Legacy Contacts, and put shared photos and domains into joint ownership. Then borrow SRE's **game day**. Once a year the agent simulates the death: the heir tries to reach the ten root accounts using only what the plan gives them, and the agent scores the life's **bus factor**. After a real death the agent never logs in as the deceased. It acts for the executor through each provider's official bereavement channel.

**MVP.** An app reads a Bitwarden or 1Password export (account names only, no secrets) and scans one mailbox for receipts. It builds the recovery graph, configures the Google, Apple and Meta legacy settings, and runs one drill.
**Kill metric:** fewer than 30% of users finish a drill.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
**Taking the game day, and making it the source of authority.** RUFADAA gives a fiduciary the *contents* of an account only if the user consented, through the provider's tool or a will. So every drill ends by signing a dated **consent schedule**: one line per account naming the heir, the scope (contents, catalogue, or delete unread) and the disposition. The will incorporates the latest schedule by reference. At death, the executor reaches each bereavement channel with consent already on paper. The drill stops being homework and becomes how the paperwork gets made.

**The honeypot, solved with social-recovery wallets (unseeded).** Argent-style Ethereum wallets already solved "let my people in without one stealable key." The map holds topology only and is encrypted on the owner's devices. Heirs are **guardians**, each holding a Shamir share, and any two can reconstruct it. Reconstruction starts a 14-day timelock that pings every device the owner has, and any tap from the owner vetoes it. A thief has to compromise two relatives and also outlast a living owner. A dead owner never vetoes, and that silence is the death proof. That replaces death verification.

**Emergent behaviour: deaths cascade.** Run drills across a household and the graphs interlock. Dad's email recovers Mum's bank, and both route through the daughter's phone plan. The real metric is the **household bus factor**: whose death locks out the most of the family. That person drains first.

**Second-order effect: a bereavement scorecard.** Each drill and each real drain logs days, documents and failures per provider. Pooled and anonymized, that data ranks companies by how hard they make it to bury someone, the way EFF's "Who Has Your Back" ranked privacy. Laggards get pressure to ship Legacy Contacts, so the tombstone improves the industry's bereavement channels.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**Kills and fixes.**
- *Silence as death proof.* Silence also means a coma, dementia, a stroke ward or a lost phone. The timelock stops thieves; it doesn't prove death. Silence triggers **incapacity mode** only: cordon, freeze new mandates, and hand off to whoever holds the durable power of attorney. Deletion and transfer need a death certificate. With dementia, incapacity is the bigger product.
- *Incorporation by reference.* It only reaches documents that existed when the will was signed, so a yearly schedule would need a yearly codicil. Skip the will: RUFADAA accepts consent in "a will, trust, power of attorney, or other record." The signed schedule is that record.
- *Heirs as the only guardians.* Elder financial abuse comes mostly from family. Use 2-of-3 instead, with the third share held by Tombstone or the family's lawyer and released only against a verified certificate.

**Household graph.** It can expose a spouse's hidden accounts, which matters most in abusive homes. Each person sees only the edges that cross into their own accounts.

**Money.** People won't pay up front for death prep; most US adults have no will. Sell through estate-planning platforms (Trust & Will, Wealth.com) and life insurers, who want beneficiaries engaged before a claim. About $60 a year per household, or a per-policy fee.

**Stack.** Takeout and IMAP parsed on the device, since a hosted Gmail scan triggers Google's restricted-scope audit. libsodium Shamir; ESIGN signatures. Legacy settings use guided deep links, not a bot driving the UI. Hard rule: no griefbot built from the data.

**90 days.** Weeks 1–4: graph and drill. Weeks 5–7: guardians, timelock, counsel review in three states. Weeks 8–10: 200 households via one estate-planning partner. Weeks 11–12: executor mode for 20 providers, plus scorecard v0.
**Kill metrics:** under 30% finish a drill, or partner conversion under 5%.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
**Final form.** Tombstone decommissions a household's digital life, sold through estate planners and life insurers. While you live, it maps the **recovery graph** on-device and moves you off fragile roots: SMS 2FA becomes passkeys, Legacy Contacts get set, shared photos and domains go into joint ownership. A yearly **game day** has an heir try to reach the root accounts and scores the **household bus factor**. Each drill signs a consent schedule, a RUFADAA "other record" naming the heir, scope and disposition for every account. The map is split 2-of-3: two heirs, plus Tombstone or counsel, whose share is released only against a certificate. Silence starts only **incapacity mode**: cordon, freeze, hand off to the power-of-attorney holder. Only a death certificate starts the **drain**, which runs from the leaves inward through official bereavement channels and ends with a tombstone notice to brokers. I accept every kill; incapacity matters most, since the graph serves the dementia decade before the funeral.

**Moonshot (2036).** "Principal status" becomes a protocol field. Electronic death and incapacity registrations publish signed events, and providers subscribe to them the way they subscribe to card-revocation lists. Providers read machine-readable consent schedules and drain their own nodes in days, not months. Every AP2 mandate and agent identity carries a liveness reference, so a dead principal's agents cordon themselves within the hour. The scorecard grows into regulation: a "right to be buried" alongside the right to be forgotten.

**Scores.** Innovation **3**. SRE drain semantics, social-recovery cryptography and RUFADAA consent records all exist. What's new is the recovery graph and the drill that produces legal authority as a by-product. That fits a mild seed. Feasibility **6**. The software is simple. The pace is set by state legal review, partner distribution, and getting people to rehearse their own death yearly.

### Turn 6 — Builder
Innovation 3 and feasibility 6 stand. Each part exists somewhere: bereavement concierges, social-recovery wallets, RUFADAA consent. The real invention is the recovery graph, plus a drill that turns a morbid chore into the paperwork that gives the executor authority. The biggest risk isn't technical. It's whether anyone rehearses their own death every year, which is why incapacity mode and distribution through insurers carry the business.

# Idea 32 — Ascension
> Seed: 32. [bold] **Roguelite × personal finance × behavioral economics.** Your actual month, played as a run.

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Ascension

**Hook:** Your month as a Slay the Spire run on a real debit card: relics are enforced rules, the death screen names the bias that killed you, and every win raises your savings rate.

**The isomorphism.** Behavioral economics already designed a roguelite. The **fresh start effect** (Dai, Milkman & Riis) is the run reset. **Commitment devices** are relics. **Save More Tomorrow** (Thaler & Benartzi) is the Ascension ladder: people refuse to save more today but accept automatic escalation later, exactly how players opt into harder Ascension levels one at a time.

**Core loop. The verb is draft.**
1. **Draft (payday).** Pick one of three relics. Each is a rule enforced at card authorization. *Payday Moat:* discretionary swipes over $50 wait 48 hours after payday. *Windfall Jar:* refunds and gifts get swept to savings, which fights mental accounting. *Latte Tithe:* every third café swipe sends the same amount to your goal. Relics stack like Balatro jokers.
2. **Map.** The calendar becomes the map. Rent is the boss. Irregular bills mined from last year's transactions are elites. Social invites are "?" nodes where you choose your path.
3. **HP is runway in days**, not dollars. Each purchase costs days of freedom.
4. **Death screen.** Finish below your floor and you get a cause of death with evidence: "Payday effect: 41% of discretionary spending within 72 hours of the deposit." That diagnosis unlocks a counter-relic for the next run.
5. **Ascend.** Each run you survive adds 1% of pay to your automatic savings. Losing never moves you down.

**Why now.** Slay the Spire 2's record 574K concurrent players made relics and Ascension a mass literacy (§4). AP2 mandates (§1) mean relics can bind the purchases your shopping agent makes for you too.

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** The relics exist already, without a game around them. Qapital's "Guilty Pleasure" rule is the Latte Tithe, and it has run since 2015. YNAB's "Age of Money" is HP measured in days. 401(k) auto-escalation is Save More Tomorrow in production. Long Game gamified savings with prize draws and was absorbed by Truebill. Digit was fined $2.7M by the CFPB in 2022 because its automatic transfers overdrew customers. That fine is the warning label on "losing never moves you down."

**Hardest problem: the game can't bind anyone.** A relic enforced at card authorization covers one card, and a present-biased player taps the other one. Commitment devices with free exits do little, and the people who need them most are the least likely to pick binding relics. Issuing your own card also means a sponsor bank, and banking-as-a-service has been under regulators' scrutiny since Synapse collapsed in 2024.

**Twist: enforce at the source.** Every card is paid from the paycheck, so the game should sit upstream of the cards. A deposit switch (Atomic or Pinwheel) routes pay into the game's FDIC-insured account. The run's gold is what the game releases into checking over FedNow, on the schedule the relics define. *Weekly Stipend* cuts pay into four draws, which flattens the payday effect for every card at once. Escape stays instant and costs no money: an emergency draw puts a **Curse** in your deck for the rest of the run. That hits score, never fees, and the death screen counts every curse. The game never sweeps a dollar it can't prove is surplus, because the Digit lesson is a hard rule.

**MVP.** Plaid read-only plus a deposit split of 20% of pay into a partner account. Three release relics, one curse type, and a month-end death screen.
**Kill metric:** fewer than 40% of players start a second run.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
**Taking the twist: the game is a reservoir.** Once pay flows through the game, the model isn't a budget but dam operation (unseeded: hydrology), a century of practice releasing lumpy inflow under uncertainty. A paycheck is the snowmelt pulse, checking is the river downstream, and the game account is the reservoir. Releases follow a **rule curve**, which keeps flood space empty ahead of forecast elites: car registration, insurance renewal, December. Like Lake Mendocino's forecast-informed operations (FIRO), releases adjust to your mined bill calendar. That also answers Digit precisely: "surplus" is whatever sits above the rule curve, and nothing is swept below it.

**Binding, paid for with yield instead of willpower.** Each run opens with a Neow-style trade. *Lock this tranche for 21 days and it earns the account's full yield. Leave it liquid and it earns nothing.* Emergency draws stay instant but carry a Curse. The evidence supports this: in the SEED trial (Ashraf, Karlan & Yin, 2006), only about 28% took the locked account, yet the group offered it saved 81% more than controls. Not everyone needs to bind; binding needs to be a draftable option with a price.

**Push further: the real audience has irregular income.** Salaried players only flatten a payday spike. Gig drivers, freelancers and tipped workers get a rain-fed river. For them the reservoir pays a **synthetic salary** out of chaotic inflow, and the run is about keeping that salary alive. That game is bigger and more needed.

**Second-order effect: drought warnings that beat payday lenders.** The rule curve shows the low-water day weeks ahead. Instead of the player finding it at a payday-loan counter, the game releases a bridge early and shows it as a boss foreseen. Where the need for a payday loan is *predictable*, the reservoir replaces it.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**Reservoir and rule curve stay. Kills:**
- *Yield as the price of binding.* $800 locked for 21 days at 4% earns $1.84. Nobody drafts for that. Pool the yield into **prize-linked savings** instead: each locked tranche earns tickets in a monthly draw. Save to Win credit unions proved it works, and a 2014 federal act makes it legal with a sponsoring bank or credit union. Cents become a jackpot.
- *The bridge.* Releasing money before the inflow arrives is lending, and advance rules differ by state. In v1 the bridge comes only from the player's own locked tranches, unlocked early with a Curse.
- *Synthetic salary from day one.* An empty reservoir can't smooth anything. Gig players get a fill phase of six to ten weeks. Forecast errors cost asymmetrically: under-release means missed rent. So the rule curve sets a release floor, and an on-demand override is always instant.
- *Diagnosis from one run.* That's n=1 storytelling, so the death screen labels the cause a hypothesis until three runs agree.

**Gig is the better wedge.** Platform payouts already go to any account number, so there's no payroll switch to negotiate.

**Money.** Low-income players shouldn't pay. Sell to gig platforms as driver retention, and to employers as a financial-wellness benefit, per active player per month.

**Stack.** A sponsor bank with a direct ledger such as Column;, since Synapse died of reconciliation. Plaid for reading; FedNow/RTP for releases; quantile forecasts over mined bill calendars; React Native.

**Ethics.** No paid items, no streaks that punish essentials, no lending upsell.

**90 days.** Weeks 1–4: bank partner, ledger, rule curve. Weeks 5–8: 300 drivers through one gig cooperative, fill phase. Weeks 9–12: synthetic salary live, first prize draw.
**Kill metrics:** over 5% of releases overridden in emergencies, or fewer than 40% start a second run.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
**Final form.** Ascension is a roguelite on your real income, gig workers first. Payouts land in a sponsor-bank reservoir. A **rule curve**, forecast from your mined bill calendar, releases a **synthetic salary** to checking over FedNow. It keeps a release floor and instant override. Each month is a run: draft relics (release and lock rules); rent is the boss, forecast irregular bills are elites, and HP is runway in days. Locking a tranche earns **prize-linked tickets**, so pooled yield becomes a monthly jackpot. An early unlock or an emergency draw puts a Curse in your deck and never costs a fee. The death screen offers a cause of death as a hypothesis until three runs agree. Each run you survive climbs one Ascension and raises the locked share by 1%. Platforms and employers pay per active player; players never do. I accept every kill. Prize-linked savings is better anyway: lotteries are what present-biased people actually draft.

**Moonshot (2036).** Reservoirs join into a **watershed**. Gig workers' income shocks are only partly correlated, so a cooperative of linked reservoirs pays each member a steady salary on far less buffer per head than anyone needs alone. That is income insurance run as a member-owned water district, with the rule curve as its actuarial model. The game also becomes behavioral economics' largest consenting field lab: every new relic ships as a preregistered randomized trial, tested on millions of real months instead of student lab sessions.

**Scores.** Innovation **5**. Qapital rules, Steady-style smoothing and Save to Win all exist. New: a hydrology rule curve driving a roguelite on top of paycheck routing, with binding sold as a drafted, prize-priced choice. Feasibility **5**. The software is ordinary; the bank partner, state advance rules and a willing gig cooperative set the pace.

### Turn 6 — Builder
Innovation 5 and feasibility 5 stand. The pieces are familiar. What's new is putting the game upstream of every card, treating the paycheck as a reservoir run on a rule curve, and selling commitment as a drafted, prize-priced relic rather than as willpower. My one change to the plan: start with a credit union rather than a fintech sponsor bank. Credit unions already run prize-linked savings, often serve drivers, and would carry the bank partner and the jackpot's legality in a single deal.

# Idea 33 — Minute Hand
> Seed: 33. [wild] **Quantum computing × puzzle games × algorithm discovery.** Players solve levels on real QPUs, and good solutions become research.

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Minute Hand

**Hook:** A tile-merging puzzle whose rules are a complete axiomatization of quantum mechanics. Its global high score is the date of Q-Day.

**The isomorphism.** The ZX-calculus draws quantum circuits as graphs of green and red "spiders." Its central rewrite, spider fusion, merges adjacent same-colour spiders and adds their phases. That is 2048. ZX is also **complete**, first for Clifford+T (Jeandel, Perdrix & Vilmart, 2018), then for all qubit QM: every true circuit equation is reachable by legal moves. Players write proofs, not toys. Every move is sound, so every solution is correct by construction; Foldit needed a lab to check designs, here checking is free.

**Core loop. The verb is fuse.**
1. **Level.** A real subcircuit from a published resource estimate: the modular arithmetic inside Shor's algorithm for elliptic curves.
2. **Fuse, pivot, complement.** Rewrite by dragging spiders. Score is **T-count**: T gates need magic-state distillation, which dominates fault-tolerant cost.
3. **Run.** Nightly, winning subcircuits small enough for Helios or Nighthawk run on hardware, with measured fidelity before and after: "your move made the computer 0.4% more accurate."
4. **Splice.** Improvements flow back into the full Shor-for-ECC estimate, and the **Q-Day clock** on the home screen moves.
5. **Replay.** Every winning rewrite path is a proof trace, training a search model the way Lean proofs feed AlphaProof.

**Why this is wild, honestly.** The bet is that human pattern sense finds rewrite *strategies* that searches miss, and that distilled strategies count as algorithm discovery. Quantum Moves hints the human edge fades once algorithms catch up. The clock reports either way.

**Why now.** The cost to break ECC fell to about 1,098 logical qubits (§2). Helios hit 99.9995% logical fidelity four days ago (§6). Oxford's "Quantum in Pictures" pilot taught ZX to schoolchildren.

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** The puzzle board exists. ZXLive (Oxford and Quantinuum) is a drag-the-spiders GUI for ZX proofs, and PyZX does automatic T-count reduction (Kissinger & van de Wetering, 2020). Citizen-science precedents include Foldit, Quantum Moves and Wootton's Decodoku. The opponent to respect is **AlphaTensor-Quantum** (DeepMind, 2025). It cut T-counts on these exact arithmetic circuits and rediscovered Karatsuba-like multiplication. Players are racing a reinforcement learner.

**Hardest problem: where is the human edge?** Small instances are where search is strongest. Shor-for-ECC runs to tens of millions of Toffolis, which no human can play. Two quieter traps. First, getting a circuit back out of an arbitrary ZX diagram can be #P-hard (de Beaudrap, Kissinger & van de Wetering, 2022), so a beautiful low-T diagram may never become a circuit again. Second, running a 12-qubit fragment on Helios measures physical noise, not fault-tolerant cost. That's a trophy, not evidence.

**Twist: play the instance, win the family.** Humans don't beat search at instances. They beat it at **generalization**. Each level is a parametric family, a modular adder at n = 3, 4 and 5. The player's rewrite path is recorded as a **tactic**, and the server replays it at n = 256 under the scalable ZX calculus (!-boxes). The score is T-count saved at cryptographic size, measured against the best bot. A tactic that works only at n = 4 scores nothing. The winning artifact is a strategy, not a diagram. Extractability is built in: moves that break the gflow condition grey out, so every board stays a circuit.

**MVP.** A web game on ZXLive and PyZX: 30 families from Litinski's 2023 ECC arithmetic, tactic replay at n = 256, and PyZX and AlphaTensor-Quantum baselines on the leaderboard.
**Kill metric:** within 90 days, no player tactic beats the best bot on any family at n = 256.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
**Taking tactics, and making the QPU evidence instead of a trophy.** Physical runs are trophies; run fragments at the **logical** level instead. Quantinuum showed logical non-Clifford gates fed by magic states in 2025, and Helios's roughly 48 logical qubits can run a family's n = 3 instance fault-tolerantly. The QPU then measures exactly what the score claims: magic states consumed and logical error per shot. Nightly runs check the T-count accounting on real hardware.

**The human edge, borrowed from speedrunning (unseeded).** Speedrunners settled human versus bot long ago: humans discover "tech," tool-assisted runs execute it perfectly, and both leaderboards coexist. So players don't race AlphaTensor-Quantum; they feed it. Each tactic becomes a **macro-action** in the bot's search. A player's score is how many T gates the bot saves at n = 256 *with* their tactic compared with *without* it. The human edge becomes a measurable marginal contribution; if it's zero, the kill metric fires honestly.

**Emergent: the opening book becomes algorithm discovery.** Tactics get names, the way chess openings do. When one named tactic saves T across unrelated families, say adders, multipliers and QROM lookups, it's a primitive, as Karatsuba's was. The real output is a cross-family tactic book with provenance, and the discovery claim is tested by transfer: a tactic counts only on families it never saw.

**Second-order: two clocks.** Add families from quantum chemistry, such as the FeMoco and P450 Hamiltonians. The home screen now has two hands: **Q-Day** and **Useful Day**, the first chemistry result a classical computer can't match. Arithmetic primitives are shared, so most tactics move both hands. The public sees that the date we can break elliptic curves and the date we can design catalysts are nearly the same date. Migration deadlines get read off a clock players maintain.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**Kills and fixes.**
- *Nightly logical runs to "check T accounting."* T-count is read off the circuit; no hardware needed. What hardware can test is the **cost model**: whether fewer T gates actually lower logical error on real fault-tolerant hardware, or whether CNOT depth and routing dominate. Run that monthly on Helios as calibration, on research credits.
- *Raw T-count as the score.* Magic-state cultivation (Gidney, Shutty & Jones, 2024) made T gates cheaper, so qubit-count and depth tradeoffs now matter. Score in spacetime volume from Google's Qualtran estimator, with assumptions published.
- *A date on the clock.* A date depends on hardware roadmaps, not on players, and the press will read it as a forecast. The hands should show **cost-to-break in logical-qubit-days**. A date appears only behind a tap, with roadmap assumptions stated.

**Speedrun framing kept.** Marginal contribution, with and without a tactic, is the right metric, and leave-one-out handles overlapping tactics. AlphaTensor-Quantum's training stack may not be public, so the reference bot is open: PyZX plus a Monte Carlo tree search (MCTS) we ship. Transfer families stay sealed and rotate each season so they can't leak.

**Abuse.** Bots submitting as humans: let them, on a separate tool-assisted board. Rewrites are sound, so only attribution can be faked.

**Ethics.** A leaderboard that makes breaking Bitcoin cheaper is dual-use. But attack-side estimates are already published openly, and a credible clock speeds up migration. Results go out as papers co-authored with post-quantum migration teams.

**Money.** DARPA's Quantum Benchmarking Initiative, hardware credits, and post-quantum bounty pools from crypto foundations. The tactic book feeds Quantum in Pictures classrooms.

**90 days.** Weeks 1–4: !-box replay engine, 10 adder and multiplier families, Qualtran scoring. Weeks 5–8: closed beta with 500 ZX-literate players. Weeks 9–12: first sealed transfer test, plus one Helios calibration run.
**Kill metric:** no tactic shows a positive marginal contribution on a sealed family.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
**Final form.** Minute Hand is a ZX-calculus puzzle game in which the verb is *fuse*, and what players produce is **tactics**, not diagrams. Levels are parametric families from published fault-tolerant estimates: ECC arithmetic, QROM, FeMoco and P450 kernels. You solve at n = 3–5 on a gflow-preserving board. The server replays your rewrite path at n = 256 with !-boxes, adds it as a macro-action to an open PyZX+MCTS bot, and scores you on the **marginal spacetime volume** the bot saves with your tactic compared with without it, using Qualtran with published assumptions. Named tactics enter an opening book. A tactic is promoted to *primitive* only after it saves volume on sealed families it never saw. Two hands, Q-Day and Useful Day, show cost in logical-qubit-days, with a date one tap away. Once a month, Helios checks whether the cost model holds on logical hardware. I accept every kill: hardware as calibration beats hardware as trophy.

**Moonshot (2036).** The tactic book *is* the compiler. Every rewrite is sound, and ZX is complete, so each tactic with an !-box is a machine-checkable theorem. The book exports to Lean as a verified rewrite library, and fault-tolerant toolchains ship it as an optimisation pass with player provenance in the changelog. The big prize is a cross-family primitive with a better *asymptotic* cost, a Karatsuba of fault-tolerant arithmetic found by a fourteen-year-old. Migration deadlines and chemistry funding calls cite the clock as public infrastructure.

**Scores.** Innovation **8**. ZXLive, PyZX and Foldit exist. What's new is crowd discovery of proof *strategies*, scored by their measured marginal value to a search bot, feeding a civic clock. Feasibility **5**. The stack is open and buildable now. Whether people have a real edge at all is a coin-flip, and the kill metric checks it.

### Turn 6 — Builder
Innovation 8 holds, though Foldit and Quantum Moves already proved the crowd-science format. What earns the wild score is scoring human strategies by their measured marginal value to an open search bot, and requiring a tactic to transfer to sealed families before it counts as a discovery. I'm cutting feasibility to 4. Lifting a rewrite path from n = 3–5 to an !-box tactic that holds at n = 256 is program synthesis, not replay, and that engine is research-grade before any player arrives. The kill metric is honest either way: if humans add nothing, the game still ships an open benchmark and a clock nobody else maintains.

# Idea 34 — Sonotype
> Seed: 34. [bold] **Bioacoustics × collecting games × biodiversity bounties.** Catch 'em all, but it's real insect and bird calls feeding science.

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Sonotype

**Hook:** A Pokédex with two columns, *Heard* and *Named*. The rarest card is a call that no scientist has matched to a species.

**The isomorphism.** Tropical bioacoustics already has the Pokédex's seen/caught split. A **sonotype** is a distinct call whose singer is unknown. Krause's acoustic niche hypothesis (partly supported, contested) says species divide up the frequency × hour-of-night plane. That turns each habitat's binder into a Mendeleev table: the empty cells are predictions.

**Core loop. The verb is pair.**
1. **Record.** Leave a phone or AudioMoth out overnight. Perch embeddings cluster the calls into sonotypes. Known calls become *Named* cards. Unknown ones become silhouettes in *Heard*, pinned to their cell in the niche grid.
2. **Pair.** You catch a silhouette by tying it to a voucher: synced video of the animal calling, a specimen, or a playback response. Experts confirm.
3. **Bounties.** Museums, agencies and nature-credit buyers post bounties on silhouettes and on **silences**. A silence is a grid cell that the habitat predicts should be occupied but that stays empty across ten nights. That is the repeat-visit non-detection data occupancy models need and nobody collects. Here absence is a card.
4. **Provenance.** The first person to hear a call and the person who pairs it split the bounty, and the named pair goes to Xeno-canto and GBIF.

**Why the domains collide.** Collecting games are completionism engines, and bioacoustics' bottleneck is labelled insect and frog calls. Completionism aimed at blanks hits that bottleneck.

**Why now.** Perch 2.0 (Aug 2025) covers about 14,600 species across many taxa, insects included, so clustering unknown calls is cheap. The brief (§14, §16.7) shows carbon markets moving only once measurement could be trusted, and the same applies to biodiversity. Repeated nights of verified silence are a building block for that measurement.

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** Every piece except the binder. BirdNET and Merlin's Sound ID turned millions of phones into recorders. iNaturalist takes sound uploads, and Seek hands out badges. Rainforest Connection's Arbimon already clusters unknown calls into "soundscape" patterns for researchers, and Zooniverse's Bat Detective crowdsourced ultrasonic labels. XPRIZE Rainforest (2024) proved acoustic surveys at speed. Nature-credit frameworks (Verra Nature, Plan Vivo PV Nature, Wallacea Trust) want verified measurements and have no supply of them. Nobody has made the unknown call the prize.

**Hardest problem: pairing, and paid silence.** Matching an unknown nocturnal katydid to its singer takes a specialist, a torch and luck. Phones also cut off near 20 kHz, and many katydids sing above it. Then there's silence. A bounty for non-detection pays best for a dead microphone, a rain-soaked night or a recorder in a sock drawer. Absence data only means something if detection probability is known.

**Twist: the call is the Poké Ball.** Crickets, katydids and frogs do **phonotaxis**: they answer or walk toward their own species' song. So the catch mechanic is playback. A kit (AudioMoth plus a cheap IR camera and speaker) plays the silhouette's call at intervals and records whatever answers or approaches. The responder is the candidate singer, on video, timestamped and geolocated. Experts confirm a clip instead of running a field trip. Playback also fixes silence: each night the kit plays a calibration tone and a common local caller's song. A night counts as silent only if that positive control was detected. Playback is capped and limited to invertebrates and frogs, never threatened birds.

**MVP.** One tropical field station, 150 AudioMoth kits for a local naturalist club, Perch 2.0 clustering, a silhouette binder, and one museum orthopterist posting the first ten bounties.
**Kill metric:** no silhouette paired by playback and confirmed by an expert within 90 days.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
**Taking playback, and making it a challenge-response (unseeded: authentication).** A responder walking toward a call isn't proof yet. Female crickets and katydids, though, have narrow **preference functions** tuned to their own species' pulse rate. So the kit sends a challenge set: the true silhouette call, plus two decoys shifted ±30% in pulse rate, in random order. A real match answers the true call and ignores the decoys, like a login that rejects wrong passwords. Three nights of correct answers make a pairing with a measured false-positive rate. Pairing stops being luck and becomes a protocol.

**Silence becomes a relay.** A kit that hears a silhouette passes the call to the next kit in the mesh, which plays it the following night. If the second kit's positive control passes and nothing answers across repeated challenges, that is a calibrated absence, with detection probability measured rather than assumed. The call moves across the landscape, building a range map for a species that has no name yet. The edge of that map is a silence card, and an edge that moves over the years is a climate signal.

**Emergent: the Poké Ball also catches the hunter.** Parasitoid flies such as *Ormia ochracea* and gleaning bats hunt by eavesdropping on these same songs, so playback draws them in on camera too. The binder grows **edges** as well as species: who eavesdrops on whom. Interactions are far scarcer data than species lists, so they pay bigger bounties.

**Second-order: splits.** When the decoy test cleanly separates two clusters inside one Named species, that may be a cryptic species. North American *Gryllus* crickets and *Chrysoperla* lacewings were found this way, by song first. Players get a Split card, and the bounty is a type description in which the song is the primary evidence.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**Kills and fixes.**
- *Decoys at ±30%.* Pulse rate in ectotherms tracks temperature closely, and female preference shifts with it. A fixed decoy can be the true song on a cold night. Kits log temperature and offset decoys from the corrected call. Spacing is calibrated per taxon on Named species first, which is the protocol's own positive control.
- *A mesh.* AudioMoths have no radio. A cloud relay does the job: a Raspberry Pi kit with LTE, about $250. Ultrasonic speakers cost hundreds more, so v1 takes only sonotypes under 20 kHz: crickets, frogs, some katydids.
- *Moving calls across the landscape.* That broadcasts foreign songs into local populations. Relay only within one watershed, under the research permits playback already needs in protected areas.
- *Split bounties paid on a type description.* That takes years and permitted specimens (Nagoya rules abroad). Pay when a partner taxonomist confirms a split candidate by DNA barcode. Players never collect.

**Abuse.** Faked pairings use internet recordings, so the server issues a fresh challenge order each night as a nonce, and kits sign their audio with device keys, so a pre-recorded "response" can't match. A covered mic fails its positive control. The worst abuse is **poaching**: rare frogs sell in the pet trade. Sensitive taxa get 10 km grid cells, as GBIF already does.

**Money.** Play is free. Revenue comes from monitoring-as-a-service for nature-credit projects and for TNFD corporate disclosure, priced per calibrated site-night. Bounties carry a 15% fee; kits rent to clubs. GBIF and NSF grants fund the open data.

**Stack.** Perch 2.0 embeddings, HDBSCAN sonotypes, Pi Zero 2 kits with IR cameras and signed uploads, and occupancy models for detection probability.

**90 days.** Weeks 1–4: kits and challenge protocol, calibrated on five Named crickets. Weeks 5–9: 150 kits at one station. Weeks 10–12: first decoy-verified pairings and silence cards.
**Kill metric:** decoy discrimination fails on Named species.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
**Final form.** Sonotype is a two-column binder for the night chorus. Signed Pi kits with an IR camera and a speaker record overnight. Perch 2.0 and HDBSCAN split each night into *Named* cards and silhouettes pinned on a frequency × hour niche grid. To **pair** a silhouette, the kit runs a challenge-response: the true call plus temperature-corrected decoys, in a fresh order each night set by a server nonce. A responder that picks the true call three nights running goes to an expert as a candidate. A **silence** card is a challenged cell inside one watershed where the positive control passed and nothing answered. Eavesdropping flies and bats on camera become **edge** cards. A **split** candidate pays when a partner taxonomist confirms it by DNA barcode. Revenue comes from calibrated site-nights sold to nature-credit and TNFD buyers. I accept every kill. The watershed limit even helps: range maps are built from local silences, not from broadcast songs.

**Moonshot (2036).** The protocol becomes the unit of account for nature. "Playback-verified presence" and "calibrated silence" turn into standard measurement units, as tonnes of CO₂ did for carbon. Biodiversity credits are then priced on detection-corrected occupancy instead of habitat-area proxies. The unnamed-insect backlog gets a song-first pipeline, with silhouettes ranked by the bounties waiting on them. Taxonomy accepts the **acoustic holotype**, a signed, challenge-verified recording beside the barcode. Edge cards add up to a continental eavesdropper food web, and moving silences become an early climate range-shift alarm.

**Scores.** Innovation **6**. Arbimon, BirdNET and iNaturalist exist. What's new is the unknown call as the prize, phonotaxis turned into a cryptographic challenge-response, and silence made into a verified, bountied asset. Feasibility **5**. The hardware is cheap and the science of preference functions is established. Permits, enough taxonomists to review pairings, and nature-credit demand set the pace.

### Turn 6 — Builder
Innovation 6 and feasibility 5 stand. The single move that earns the score is using the animal's own preference function as the authenticator. It turns a citizen-science lucky shot into a protocol with a measured false-positive rate, and it makes silence something you can pay for without paying for broken microphones. The real constraint is the market, not the hardware. Nature-credit and TNFD demand for calibrated site-nights is still forming, so grants and a single museum partner have to carry the first two seasons.
