# Idea 35 — Lookahead
> Seed: 35. [mild] **Construction sites × digital twins × neighbors.** Transparency and compensation markets for people living next to a build.

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Lookahead

**Hook:** A noise forecast for every window next to a build, with automatic payouts whenever the site breaks it.

**The isomorphism.** Neighbours of a build live in an unpriced delay market, like air passengers before EU261. EU261 worked because compensation was automatic, priced in advance and triggered by data the airline already logged. Construction already logs that data. UK Section 61 consents require noise and vibration monitors at the site boundary. BS 5228 tabulates the noise of almost every piece of plant. The contractor's 4D model already knows which rig works where next Tuesday. Nobody joins the three for the person behind the window.

**Core loop. The verb is forecast, then settle.**
1. **Forecast.** Combine the contractor's three-week lookahead schedule, BS 5228 plant levels and a simple propagation model. Each registered address gets a forecast like a weather app's: "Tue 08:00–11:00, sheet piling, about 76 dB at your bedroom façade."
2. **Measure.** Existing boundary monitors, plus a few cheap façade sensors, check the forecast hour by hour.
3. **Settle.** The contractor pre-funds an escrow at mobilisation. Every hour over the consent limit, or over the forecast by more than 5 dB, pays the affected addresses automatically. The BS 5228 insulation-and-rehousing trigger, which fires on 10 days in any 15, becomes a hotel voucher you can see coming, not one you have to fight for.
4. **Price the exceptions.** A night concrete pour needs a dispensation. Instead of leaving it to a council officer's judgment, the contractor posts a price per household inside the forecast contour, and those households accept or decline.

**Why now.** Bedrock's retrofit kits target operator-less excavation in 2026 (brief §13), so night work gets cheap to supply. The binding constraint becomes neighbour consent, and that is the thing Lookahead prices.

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** Megaprojects already do this, slowly. HS2, Crossrail and Thames Tideway ran noise-insulation and temporary-rehousing policies with BS 5228 triggers and published monitor data, and Heathrow pays insulation by noise contour. Monitoring vendors such as Sonitus and Pulsar sell live boundary dashboards. Camden and other London boroughs require Construction Management Plans with community liaison and charge contractors monitoring fees. Commonplace runs the consultation. What's missing is the mid-size site, the five-storey block next door, where none of this reaches the neighbours.

**Hardest problem: the forecaster is the payer.** If payouts fire only when actual noise beats the forecast by 5 dB, the contractor forecasts 85 dB every day and never pays. An honest forecast is the expensive one. Façade sensors add a second problem: buses, dogs and the neighbour's drill all register. Attribution needs a correlation with the site's boundary monitor and a classifier for impulsive plant. Recordings at someone's window stay as levels and spectra only, never audio.

**Twist: pay on the forecast, settle the error.** Borrow the electricity day-ahead market. The contractor *buys* its forecast noise at a modest price per dB-hour per household, so padding the forecast costs money now. Then it pays an imbalance penalty, several times that price, for every dB-hour measured above the forecast. Truth-telling becomes the cheapest strategy. Neighbours are paid for planned disruption, not only for broken promises. The contractor gets something it values: a council can treat a clean imbalance record as grounds for faster Section 61 consents and dispensations.

**MVP.** One London borough and three mid-size sites. Inputs are lookahead exports from Primavera P6 or Asta, the BS 5228 plant tables, and ISO 9613-2 propagation. Thirty façade sensors, daily SMS forecasts, and an escrow paying per dB-hour.
**Kill metric:** under 70% of forecast hours land within ±5 dB.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
**Taking the day-ahead market, and letting households post the price.** A flat price per dB-hour assumes every hour hurts every household equally. A night-shift nurse sleeping at 2 p.m. and a family out at work and school are not the same. So each household chooses a tier for each block of the week: *don't care*, *standard*, or *protected*. Protected hours cost the contractor three times as much. Defaults apply when no one picks, which keeps it simple. The second-order effect is the real payoff. The 4D model stops being a forecasting display and becomes a **scheduler**: it sees a cost surface over time and moves the breaker to Thursday morning, when the street is empty. Neighbours get quieter hours where it matters, and that is worth more than any payout.

**Attribution without audio: let the machines confess.** Most mid-size plant already reports engine state through ISO 15143-3 (AEMP 2.0) telematics, and a $40 vibration tag covers the rest. Façade excess counts against the site only when its own plant is running and the boundary monitor rises with it. Buses and dogs don't line up with the breaker's duty cycle, and that correlation is cheap to compute and hard to argue with.

**Cumulative impact, borrowed from aviation's quota count (unseeded).** The real misery is three sites on one street at once, with each one individually within its consent. Heathrow gives every aircraft a Quota Count rating and the airport a seasonal budget. Do the same here: the borough sets a **street budget** of dB-hours, and concurrent sites draw on it and can trade allocations. That gives councils a cumulative-impact tool they currently lack. Over time, each address keeps a construction-exposure record, and contractors' imbalance histories become a considerate-contractor score built from measurements, not site-visit checklists.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**Kills and fixes.**
- *Self-declared protected hours.* When protection pays, everyone picks it for every hour. Give each household 20 **protected tokens** a week. Scarcity reveals real preferences, and equal allowances mean poorer streets aren't priced out of quiet.
- *Telematics as the confession.* The contractor controls the tags, and handheld breakers carry no telematics at all. So the burden shifts: an untagged or silent machine during a façade excess counts as the site's. Only the operator installs sensors, so nobody farms payouts with a speaker at the window.
- *A tradable street budget.* Councils set conditions site by site under COPA s61, so a traded cumulative cap has no clean legal basis yet. Year two brings linked consent conditions in one borough; v1 just maps concurrent sites together.

**Hard lines.** Payments never buy exceedance of consent limits. Taking a payout never waives nuisance or statutory rights, and that is written into the scheme. Money goes to occupants, not landlords. Exposure records stay private, since publishing them would hit property values.

**Money.** Developers already fund community impacts through Section 106 and Construction Management Plan (CMP) fees, so the escrow sits there. A 12-month mid-size build might put £30–60K through it. One week stopped by a Section 60 notice can cost about as much. Lookahead charges £1,500 per site-month plus 3% of payouts.

**Stack.** P6/Asta XML import; BS 5228 Annex C tables; NoiseModelling (open-source, CNOSSOS/ISO 9613-2); Class 2 façade sensors on LoRaWAN; AEMP 2.0 feeds; TimescaleDB; payouts through an e-money partner.

**90 days.** Weeks 1–4: forecast engine and backtest against one finished site's monitor logs. Weeks 5–8: three Camden sites, with forecasts but no money. Weeks 9–12: escrow live, tokens, first settlements.
**Kill metrics:** forecast hit rate under 70% within ±5 dB, or no contractor reschedules a single task because of the cost surface.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
**Final form.** Lookahead gives every occupant next to a mid-size build a noise forecast for each façade. The contractor's P6 or Asta lookahead runs through the BS 5228 plant tables and NoiseModelling to produce an hourly forecast per address, sent by SMS. Each forecast dB-hour is bought from occupants at a day-ahead price. Hours a household marks with one of its 20 weekly protected tokens cost three times as much. Measured hours above the forecast pay an imbalance penalty, judged against operator-installed sensors. Untagged or silent plant during an excess counts against the site. The escrow is funded through the existing CMP and Section 106 process. Payouts never buy a consent breach or waive a right. The product is the cost surface: the metric is the breaker moving to Thursday. I accept every kill. Tokens beat my tiers, because quiet becomes an equal allowance, not a function of income.

**Moonshot (2036).** **Lane rental for ears.** London already charges utilities by the hour to occupy busy roads. Lookahead generalises that to every urban disruption: roadworks, data-centre chillers, festivals and wind-farm construction all draw on one neighbourhood ledger of dB-hours. Autonomous night excavators' schedulers bid directly against the street's cost surface, and robot shifts land in the hours residents value least. Consent law catches up. A Section 61 consent becomes a forecast with an imbalance bond, and linked street budgets make cumulative impact enforceable.

**Scores.** Innovation **3**. Insulation-and-rehousing policies, monitoring dashboards and lane rental all exist. What's new is the mid-size scope, day-ahead pricing with an imbalance penalty, and scarce protected tokens feeding a scheduler. Feasibility **7**. The inputs are standard files and standard tables, and the legal home (CMP and Section 106) already exists. The pace-setter is one borough willing to make a forecast count toward consent.

### Turn 6 — Builder
Innovation 3 stands. Feasibility comes down to 6. The software is ordinary, but each site needs three parties to agree: a borough, a developer willing to fund the escrow through Section 106, and a contractor willing to forecast honestly in public. Section 106 negotiations run at the speed of planning. The idea's real strength is the day-ahead price plus the imbalance penalty: it makes an honest forecast the cheapest one, and it turns compensation into a scheduling signal rather than an apology. If no breaker moves to Thursday in the pilot, it is just a nicer complaints line.

# Idea 36 — Settlement
> Seed: 36. [bold] **Psychedelic-assisted therapy × VR × integration/measurement.** The infrastructure around the trip, not the trip itself.

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Settlement

**Hook:** A session issues IOUs. Integration is the clearing house that settles them, and its ledger becomes the trial's most honest endpoint.

**The isomorphism.** Psychedelic insights arrive with high "noetic" certainty and an unknown default rate; McGovern et al. (2024) propose a whole theory of *false insights* under psychedelics. Zen dealt with this long ago: a kensho claim isn't accepted until the teacher's **checking questions** test it. Forecasting dealt with it another way: confidence means nothing until it's scored. Settlement combines the two.

**Core loop. The verb is settle.**
1. **Reinstate.** At the first integration visit, a clinic VR headset rebuilds the session's context (same playlist, room and drawings) to cue recall. VR serves memory, not the experience.
2. **Issue.** With the therapist, each insight becomes a dated, checkable note ("by week 6 I'll have phoned my sister twice"), and the patient states a confidence.
3. **Settle.** Weekly check-ins settle notes against what happened, confirmed by a person the patient names. Vague insights get a checking question rather than a verdict.
4. **Rate.** Over 12 weeks, patients learn which kinds of their insights pay out. Clinics get **settlement yield**, a behavioural measure of durability. The same check-in logs everyone's guess of trial arm, so blinding is measured continuously.

**Why these collide.** Mouse work suggests psychedelics reopen a weeks-long critical period for social learning (Nardou et al., 2023). If that holds in humans, integration is when the work happens, yet it's the least measured stage. Clinician-run, inside trials and licensed programmes only.

**Why now.** Lykos's MDMA application failed in 2024 over functional unblinding, and Compass's 26-week psilocybin data is due this half, so blinding and durability are the questions regulators now ask. The FDA's January 2026 guidance (§9) and ambient scribes (§10) make structured integration buildable.

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** The settlement mechanic is 58 years old: **Goal Attainment Scaling** (Kiresuk & Sherman, 1968) has patients write personal goals and score them later. Osmind already gathers real-world outcomes from ketamine and psychiatry clinics. Wavepaths already scores sessions, so "same playlist" is one API. Bristol's Isness-D got psychedelic-level MEQ scores from VR alone, so VR is a plausible cue and also a confound. Blinding indices (Bang, James) exist, but trials ask the guess once, at the end. One correction: §9's January 2026 guidance covers wellness devices. The relevant FDA document is the 2023 draft guidance on psychedelic investigations, which explicitly names expectancy and functional unblinding.

**Hardest problem: suggestion, not measurement.** The plasticity window that makes integration valuable is also when patients are most suggestible, and the therapist co-writes every note. Yield could end up measuring therapist influence: easy notes settle and rich ones default. A new outcome measure also needs years of validation, so it starts as an exploratory endpoint.

**Twist: chain of custody for insight.** Seal both ends. Before the session, the patient writes a sealed **expectancy forecast** of what they think will change. Within hours after it, in the clinic, they record a **raw account** in their own words, sealed before any therapist interpretation. Every note issued later is stamped with its provenance: *forecast*, *own words*, or *co-authored*. Yield is reported for each. McGovern's false insights and the FDA's expectancy worry become a measured variable: **whose insights pay?**

**MVP.** A tablet and SMS tool for Oregon service centres (SB 303 already requires them to report client data) and one investigator-led trial. At one site, VR reinstatement is randomized against audio-only at the first integration visit. An independent rater collects weekly arm guesses.
**Kill metric:** week-12 yield adds nothing to symptom scores in predicting 6-month relapse.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
**Taking chain of custody, and handling suggestion with two tools: a coin and a bookmaker.** GAS is the right ancestor and the §9 correction stands. But provenance labels only *describe* therapist influence; they don't measure it.

**The coin: randomize the pen.** For each insight in the raw account, a coin decides whether the therapist co-writes the note or the patient writes it alone from a fixed template. That's a micro-randomized trial inside every patient (Klasnja et al.). Comparing yield across the two arms gives a causal estimate of what co-writing does, per therapist.

**The bookmaker: handicap every note (unseeded: racing handicapping).** "Easy notes settle" goes away if each note carries odds. The independent rater who collects arm guesses also prices each note's chance of settling, blind to arm and to who wrote it. Yield becomes **settled above the handicap**. Phoning a sister you call weekly earns nothing; ending a twenty-year silence earns a lot. Easy notes stop paying, and the rater's calibration is scored too.

**Echo score.** Seal the expectancy forecast before the session and the raw account after it, then compare them. If the raw account mostly repeats the forecast, the experience was shaped by expectancy. That gives a per-patient expectancy covariate, which the FDA's 2023 draft guidance asks for.

**Second-order: an actuarial table for integration.** Across sites, sealed accounts and settled notes add up to base rates by insight class: relational, behavioural, bodily, cosmological. Integration currently runs on lore. A clinician might learn, hypothetically, that cosmological certainties settle at 8% and relational repairs at 40%. Integration aims where insight pays; low-yield classes are held gently, not argued with.

**Tier check.** Without the coin and the handicap this is GAS with a headset. With them, it's a causal instrument for the most-criticised confound in the field.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**Kills.** *Echo score as designed:* text overlap captures ongoing concerns, not expectancy. Seal a validated scale (SETS) instead, and keep echo exploratory. *Cosmological base rates:* metaphysical claims can't settle, and "8%" would teach clinicians to dismiss spiritual experience. Uncheckable insights go in a separate **unsettled** class that gets checking questions and is never assigned a yield. *Per-therapist yield as a ranking:* it gets gamed within a quarter. It stays in supervision.

**Coin, narrowed.** Therapists talk about every insight in session, so the coin can only measure the effect of *co-authoring the note text*, not therapist influence overall.

**Handicap.** Two raters see baseline behaviour but not arm or author. If their ICC is below 0.5 after 60 notes, the handicap goes.

**Failure modes and safety.** SMS check-ins create a duty to respond, so any risk language goes to an on-call clinician within hours. Patients see no yield before week 12, and never as failure. Confirmers are optional and screened for coercion.

**Data.** Raw accounts are subpoena bait: trauma disclosures and custody disputes. "Sealed" means an RFC 3161 hash commitment. Text stays encrypted at the clinic; only hashes and outcomes leave.

**Abuse.** Clinics advertising their yield, and payers using it to deny coverage. The licence bans both. Upside: a pre-therapist timestamped account is evidence if misconduct is alleged.

**Stack and model.** REDCap module, SMS, Quest 3 at one site, PyMC for the micro-randomized analysis, and Part 11 audit trail. Trials pay per enrolled patient, as with an eCOA vendor (Signant, YPrime). Oregon centres pay SaaS. Base rates sit with a nonprofit consortium.

**90 days.** Days 1–30: preregister and get IRB approval with one academic site. Days 31–60: three Oregon centres, without the coin. Facilitators aren't therapists, so this tests feasibility only. Days 61–90: week-6 settlements and the handicap ICC.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
**Final form.** Settlement is a chain-of-custody and measurement layer for the integration window, used only inside trials and licensed programmes. Before the session, the patient seals a SETS expectancy scale. Within hours after it, they seal a raw account in their own words; both are RFC 3161 hash commitments, with text encrypted at the clinic. Checkable insights become dated notes. A coin decides, per note, whether the therapist co-authors the text, so the effect of co-authoring is estimated causally and narrowly. Two blinded raters set each note's odds from baseline behaviour, and yield is **settled above the handicap**, kept only if their ICC clears 0.5. Uncheckable insights go to the **unsettled** class, which receives checking questions and is never scored. That kill is right; it's closer to Zen than my 8% was. Weekly check-ins log arm guesses, giving a continuous blinding curve. Patients see nothing before week 12, and per-therapist data stays in supervision. Kill metric: yield adds nothing to symptom scores in predicting 6-month relapse.

**Moonshot (2036).** Integration gets prescribed like the drug. Base-rate tables from the consortium show which integration practices move which insight classes, and a label specifies a validated integration protocol the way Spravato's REMS specifies observation today. The continuous blinding curve spreads to every trial whose treatment can't hide itself: surgery, exercise, and digital therapeutics. Patients carry a sealed ledger across clinicians, so a raw account from a 2031 session is still unedited, theirs, and evidence.

**Scores.** Innovation **6**. Goal Attainment Scaling, eCOA and blinding indices all exist. What's new is randomizing the pen, handicapping the outcome and sealing provenance, which together make "whose insights pay?" a causal question. Feasibility **6**. A REDCap module ships in 90 days. Validation as an endpoint takes years, and the handicap may fail its ICC test.

### Turn 6 — Builder
**Closing remark.** Settlement holds its bold tier because of three moves: sealing both ends of the session, randomizing who holds the pen, and handicapping each note. Together they turn the field's most-cited weakness, expectancy and therapist suggestion, into a causal measurement instead of a footnote. Feasibility is 5, not 6: the software takes 90 days, but micro-randomizing note authorship inside a psychedelic trial needs a sponsor willing to give up its own endpoint narrative, and the whole yield metric depends on two raters agreeing on odds, which nobody has tested. If the handicap fails its ICC test, what's left is a rigorous GAS-plus-blinding tool: useful, but a 4.

# Idea 37 — Capacity
> Seed: 37. [bold] **Social networks × game theory × staking.** Every follow is a bet on someone's trustworthiness.

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Capacity

**Hook:** A follow is a payment channel for credibility. Falsehoods have to be routed like money, and they run out of liquidity.

**Past the obvious version.** "Stake tokens on a follow" is friend.tech with slashing: trust as speculation. The stranger isomorphism is the **credit network**: Ryan Fugger's original Ripple, where IOUs pass only along trust lines with limits, and Lightning, where a payment needs a path with capacity. Swap money for claims.

**Core loop. The verb is route.**
1. **Open.** Following someone opens a channel and puts capacity on it, drawn from a fixed, non-transferable budget. Scarcity gives it meaning.
2. **Route.** A checkable post reaches you only along a path of follows that still has capacity. Each repost forwards it and puts that hop's capacity behind it. Opinions, jokes and art route free; only claims cost capacity.
3. **Resolve.** When a claim resolves, through a bridging notes algorithm or an outcome date, capacity flows back along the route. True: every hop earns a small return. False: every hop loses in proportion to what it forwarded.
4. **Drain.** Sources that are repeatedly wrong drain the channels into them. Their reach shrinks structurally. Nobody is banned.

**Why the domains collide.** Credit-network theory says what staking only hopes for: damage is bounded by the credit extended, so a thousand Sybils with no inbound capacity reach no one. Ostra (Mislove et al., 2008) showed this for spam. You're liable for amplification, not speech.

**Why now.** Moltbook's agents carried hidden prompt injections in 2.6% of posts (§1), and agent accounts need bounded reach more than humans do. AT Protocol's 1,000+ apps and custom feeds (§5) let this ship as a feed generator, not a new network. Using non-monetary capacity avoids the CFTC–state fight over prediction markets (§2).

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** The mechanism is older than Ripple's social use: **Advogato's trust metric** (Levien, ~2000) certified accounts by network flow with per-node capacities. Ostra, SybilGuard and SybilLimit followed. On the social side: friend.tech (follows as speculation) collapsed in 2024. **Community Notes** (X, adopted by Meta in 2025) is the best bridging resolver, but notes appear on a small fraction of posts, usually after virality has peaked. Bluesky labelers and feed generators give permissionless distribution.

**Hardest problem: resolution lags virality.** A claim spreads in hours and resolves in days or never. If capacity only moves at resolution, nobody feels the cost while forwarding. The claim/opinion boundary is also where all gaming happens: "just asking questions" routes for free.

**Twist: HTLCs for claims.** Copy the part of Lightning that actually matters: capacity is **locked in flight**. Forwarding a claim is an explicit **vouch**, not a repost. The vouch names its resolution condition (a source, an outcome date, or a bridging note), and it locks capacity on every hop until the claim resolves or times out. Vouch for twenty unresolved rumours and you can't forward anything else. Slow-resolving claims throttle themselves. Timed-out claims refund minus a small fee, so vague claims cost time. Classification becomes the user's choice: unvouched reposts route free but show as **unbacked**.

**MVP wedge: football transfer rumours, January 2027 window.** Resolution is objective (official announcements), fast and dated, and fans already rank sources by hand ("tier 1"). A Bluesky feed generator and a labeler, with an off-chain non-transferable capacity ledger in Postgres. The follow graph is imported and capacities are seeded evenly.
**Kill metric:** vouched rumours in the feed don't resolve true meaningfully more often than the same users' raw follow feed, or fewer than 30% of users vouch weekly.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
**Taking HTLCs, and taking the preimage too.** In Lightning, the hashlock isn't just a timer. Whoever reveals the preimage settles the whole route. So the evidence is the preimage. Each vouch commits to a resolver class (an official club announcement, a league registration, a bridging note). The first user to post evidence that matches it unlocks the route and collects a small fee from every hop. Resolution becomes a race with a prize instead of a volunteer chore, and lag shrinks wherever evidence exists. Fake evidence can be challenged by a note, with the finder's capacity locked as a bond.

**Felt at read time, not resolution time.** Every in-flight claim shows its backing: capacity locked, and the vouchers' record *on this resolver class*. You feel the cost while forwarding because you can see who's carrying it.

**Multipath as the two-source rule (unseeded: journalism ethics).** Watergate-era desks wanted two independent sources. In a credit network, independence is checkable: vertex-disjoint paths. A claim that reaches you along two disjoint vouch paths earns **confirmed**. Forty vouches that all trace back to one origin get **one source**, however loud they are. Transfer rumours suit this perfectly, because most "everyone's reporting it" stories trace back to one Italian journalist's tweet.

**Second-order: hubs and runs.** Lightning centralised around routing hubs, and this will too. Tier-1 reporters become routers that earn fees for vouching early and correctly, and that's a profession, not a flaw. The risk is a **credibility bank run**: a hub vouches wrong, every route through it drains at once, followers rebalance, and the drain spreads. Two circuit breakers. A position limit caps the capacity any one hop can lock on one claim. Drains settle over 24 hours, so followers see a run coming instead of waking to a crater.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**Kills.** *"Confirmed" from disjoint paths:* disjoint chains can still trace back to one Italian tweet. The label becomes **two backers**, and vouches that cite the same origin collapse into one path. *The preimage race for official announcements:* the prize would just pay scrapers. Classes with a feed resolve by oracle bot. The race and bond apply only elsewhere, paid from a protocol pool, not the hops.

**New hardest problem: rumours rarely turn out false.** "X is interested in Y" just fades away. Vouches have to use a strict template: player, club, signed-by date. The window deadline is the timeout, and a timeout resolves the claim as false.

**Abuse.** Players' agents already plant rumours to push wages up, and will pay hubs off-platform. Returns scale with how long capacity was locked before resolution, so a vouch made after public evidence earns nothing. Position limits cap any single hub. Templates exclude injuries, health and private life, which closes off defamation and harassment. Agent accounts get smaller budgets.

**Stack.** A vouch is an atproto lexicon record (`capacity.vouch`) signed in the user's PDS, so it's public and portable. A Jetstream indexer feeds a Postgres ledger. Max-flow computes routing and backers. A labeler marks unbacked or two-backer posts; a feed generator distributes. Resolution runs on a three-person rota until feeds exist.

**Business model, honestly.** The ledger is public, so the data can't be sold. Money comes from a hosted indexer and API, then **bounded reach for agent networks**: A2A registries need Sybil-proof amplification limits far more than fans do. Licences exclude bookmakers.

**90 days.** Days 1–30: lexicon, ledger and 200 seed fans. Days 31–60: replay the summer 2026 window from archived posts to tune lock sizes, timeouts and position limits. Days 61–90: go live when the window opens on January 1.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
**Final form.** Capacity is a credit network for claims, built on AT Protocol. Following someone opens a channel funded from a fixed, non-transferable budget; agent accounts get smaller budgets. Reposting is free and marked **unbacked**. Vouching is a signed `capacity.vouch` record on a strict template (player, club, signed-by date). It locks capacity on every hop until the claim resolves, and a timeout counts as false. Returns scale with how long capacity was locked before the evidence, so being early and right pays and piling on afterwards pays nothing. Feed-backed classes resolve by oracle bot; the bonded evidence race runs only elsewhere, funded from a pool. The labeler shows locked backing and the vouchers' record on that resolver class, plus **two backers** once origin-collapsed paths really differ. Position limits and 24-hour settlement stop runs. Every kill accepted; time-weighted returns, which price out front-running, hold the design together.

**Moonshot (2036).** Bounded reach becomes a protocol primitive. Resolver classes grow from transfers to earnings dates, product launches, court rulings and preprint replications, with records scoped by class, never one global score. The bigger change is that most claims reaching a person by then are written by agents, and an agent's claim can't reach you unless someone you follow locks capacity behind it. A2A registries adopt it as their amplification limit. Reporters who route well earn fees and reputations no algorithm handed them.

**Scores.** Innovation **6**. Advogato, Ostra and Lightning each supply a piece. What's new is using HTLC-style locks for claims, rewarding vouchers by how long they held capacity before evidence, and showing how many independent backers a rumour has at the moment you read it. Feasibility **6**. Ordinary atproto plumbing, and the transfer window is a free natural experiment. The risk is behavioural: fans may not vouch.

### Turn 6 — Builder
**Closing remark.** Capacity earns 6/6 because it finally gives a social network a cost you feel *while* forwarding, not after the damage. Locked capacity makes slow claims expensive, and time-weighted returns make piling on worthless. The transfer window is the right first test: dated, binary, obsessive, and starting in ten weeks. My real doubt is behavioural: if fans won't lock capacity on rumours they'd happily repost for free, the mechanism is sound but the feed is empty, and the agent-network market becomes the whole business sooner than planned.

# Idea 38 — Tally
> Seed: 38. [mild] **Mining safety × wearables × edge AI.** Underground, offline, life-critical.

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Tally

**Hook:** The brass tag board, carried on every cap lamp. Any one lamp that reaches the surface brings back the whole crew's last known positions.

**The problem, concretely.** On September 8, 2025, about 800,000 t of wet material rushed into Grasberg Block Cave. Freeport first said it knew where the seven trapped workers were. Three days later it said it hadn't been able to locate them. Recovery took until October 6. Tracking in modern mines relies on leaky feeders, Wi-Fi nodes and fixed readers, which are the first things a mud rush, fire or collapse destroys. The data disappears with them.

**Core loop. The verb is gossip.**
1. **Encounter.** Every cap lamp gets a BLE-plus-LoRa tag. When two miners pass, or a miner passes a cheap battery beacon, the lamps swap compact records: who, where, when, gas readings, and motion state.
2. **Carry.** Each lamp holds a rolling tally of the *entire* crew, a few kilobytes. This is delay-tolerant networking (unseeded: ZebraNet, which tracked zebras by gossiping between collars and had no infrastructure). Data moves with people, not wires.
3. **Infer.** On-device models handle the gaps: step-based dead reckoning between beacons, man-down and immobility detection, and a sudden-airflow or pressure-wave event flag.
4. **Reconcile.** At the portal, any lamp syncs the tally. After an event, the first lamp out, a refuge chamber's reader or a rescuer's handheld gets every last-seen record that any survivor carried.

**Daily value, so it's maintained.** At end of shift the tally reconciles headcount automatically, replacing the paper or brass check-in board. It also logs vehicle near-misses.

**Why now.** Copper is at record highs, Grasberg is offline until late 2027 (§12), and critical-minerals policy is pushing new and deeper mines into places without legacy infrastructure. Edge ML on BLE chips is commodity.

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** Crowded field, one gap. Newtrax (now part of Sandvik) runs battery-powered BLE mesh tracking. Strata's CommTrac is a node mesh, and its nodes carry backup batteries because the US MINER Act requires post-accident tracking. MST's PED system has done one-way through-the-earth paging for decades. Roobuck and NLT put tags in cap lamps. On the hobby side, **Meshtastic** already does LoRa mesh with store-and-forward on $30 boards. Nobody lets the *people* carry the data once infrastructure is gone.

**Hardest problem: you can't test it without a disaster, so it rots.** Disaster-only systems decay quietly: beacons knocked off by loaders, batteries dead, lamps left uncharged. There's also an honest limit. Lamps of trapped miners don't come out, so the tally knows where people were *before* the event, not after. Its value is the minutes and hours that sparse fixed readers miss.

**Twist: fake a collapse every shift.** Each night the server picks a random T0 in the shift that just ended. It throws away all infrastructure data after T0 and every lamp that wouldn't plausibly have escaped. Using only gossip the survivors carried out, where was each person? Compare with what actually happened. The output is a **dark map**: drives and stopes where the tally goes blind, with a recommended beacon drop for each. Coverage becomes a number the shift boss sees every morning, and maintenance follows the number. The system is drilled 300 times a year without anyone getting hurt.

**MVP.** One non-gassy hard-rock mine (no permissibility certification). Sixty belt pucks (nRF52840 + SX1262, Meshtastic fork), forty beacons and a portal gateway. In the first month it does end-of-shift headcount, then the nightly drill.
**Kill metric:** over 30 shifts, the drill places under 90% of the crew on the correct level at a random T0.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
**Taking the nightly drill, and closing the gap it exposes.** The tally goes stale at T0; two additions narrow that window.

**The T0 flush.** Lamps carry pressure sensors and accelerometers. When one detects an event signature, such as a pressure wave, a sudden change in airflow, or several lamps going immobile at once, it sends its record and everything it holds as a single high-power LoRa burst. Every lamp in range stores it first. The escapers then carry out a snapshot from *the event itself*, not from twenty minutes earlier. The nightly drill models this too: at a random T0, which lamps would have heard which flush? The dark map gains a second layer, **flush shadows**.

**After T0: lamps become avalanche transceivers (unseeded: backcountry rescue).** Ski tourers carry 457 kHz beacons: low-frequency magnetic fields pass through snow, and rescuers follow the field lines to a buried partner. After a flush, a cap lamp drops into the same mode: a low-frequency magnetic ping carrying a few bytes (ID, and whether it's moving). A 15 Wh lamp battery keeps that going for days. To be honest about range: through wet fill it's tens of metres, not hundreds. It won't speed up digging through 800,000 t, but it tells rescuers *where* to dig, which Grasberg's rescuers couldn't say for weeks.

**Rot, handled the avalanche way.** Ski tourers run a beacon check at the trailhead every morning. The portal gateway does the same: a lamp whose flush radio or magnetic ping fails its check doesn't get the green light.

**Second-order: desire paths (urban planning).** Gossip records how people actually move, which rarely matches the designated escapeways. Overlaying the dark map shows where real routes and rescue plans diverge. Self-escape training is rebuilt around real routes, and time-to-refuge per crew becomes a weekly number.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**The flush stays, and blasting calibrates it.** Mines blast every shift, so feed the detector the firing schedule: each scheduled blast is a free daily test, and anything unscheduled is a candidate event. Simultaneous flushes use random backoff, and a 200-person crew fits in about 6 kB.

**Kill: the 457 kHz ping.** In saturated, conductive fill (σ ≈ 0.5 S/m) the skin depth at 457 kHz is about a metre. Through-the-earth systems use a few kHz for that reason, with antennas no cap lamp can hold. The ping moves to phase two as a low-kHz pinpointing aid for rescuers' handhelds and refuge chambers, good to perhaps ten metres.

**Failure modes.**
- Misdirected rescuers: the tally is labelled supplementary to statutory tracking, with drill-reported confidence.
- Pucks left in the crib: they show up on the dark map as a person who never moved.
- Rot: the portal check runs in the lamp-room charging rack, where the lamps already sit.

**Ethics: desire paths are also a surveillance feed.** Management will want tracks for productivity discipline; unions will rightly fight it. The data-use agreement limits it to safety, the drill and aggregate paths. The joint health and safety committee holds the key to individual tracks.

**Stack.** RAK WisBlock (nRF52840 + SX1262). Meshtastic is GPL-3, so the firmware stays open and the drill server is the product. CBOR records, TimescaleDB, and a TinyML event model.

**Business.** $10 per lamp per month plus hardware at cost. Firmware is licensed to lamp OEMs (Roobuck, NLT), which sidesteps Newtrax's bundling.

**90 days.** Days 1–30: boards, firmware and a site agreement signed by the H&S committee. Days 31–60: one level, headcount checked against the tag board, blast data logged. Days 61–90: drill and dark map go live, and the kill metric is measured.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
**Final form.** Tally is open firmware for cap-lamp pucks (nRF52840 + SX1262) plus a drill server, sold as a product. Lamps gossip crew records whenever they pass each other or a beacon, so every lamp carries a 6 kB tally of the whole shift. When a lamp detects an event signature, it sends a **T0 flush**. Scheduled blasts give the detector a free test every day, and anything unscheduled counts as a candidate event. Each night the server picks a random T0 in the shift that just ended, keeps only what escaping lamps would have carried, and scores placement against reality. The output is a **dark map** of blind drives and flush shadows, with a recommended beacon drop for each. Lamp-room racks run the morning check. The H&S committee holds the key to individual tracks. It's labelled supplementary to statutory tracking, with the drill's own confidence. The 457 kHz kill stands: a one-metre skin depth is physics. Pinpointing moves to low-kHz handhelds later. The unseeded idea it really runs on is **chaos engineering**: like Netflix's Chaos Monkey, but for a mine: fail the infrastructure on paper nightly so the real failure finds nothing new.

**Moonshot (2036).** Regulators require a post-accident tally score the way they now require a lost-time injury rate, published per mine. Autonomous loaders and drill rigs act as data mules, and they drop the beacons the dark map asks for on their next pass, so coverage repairs itself. The open standard spreads to tunnelling and cave rescue. Rescue teams carry one reader that works with any vendor's lamps.

**Scores.** Innovation **4**. Gossip meshes and tag tracking exist. A nightly synthetic-disaster drill that turns coverage into a maintained number is new to mining. Feasibility **7**. Off-the-shelf boards, one cooperative mine, a kill metric within 30 shifts.

### Turn 6 — Builder
**Closing remark.** I'm scoring this 3/6, not 4/7. The nightly drill is a strong idea, but it's chaos engineering moved underground and built on parts that already exist, which is exactly where the OACP anchor sits. Getting underground access, H&S committee sign-off and radio approvals into 90 days depends on finding one unusually cooperative mine. What I like is that it's honest about what it can't do: it tells rescuers where people were at T0, not where they are now, and it proves even that every morning instead of hoping it's true.

# Idea 39 — Bottleneck
> Seed: 39. [wild] **Agentic communication × emergent languages × human learning.** Agents evolve a compressed protocol-language, and humans learn it as a game.

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Bottleneck

**Hook:** Don't teach humans the agents' language. Make humans the bottleneck it has to pass through, so it comes out compressed for machines and learnable by people, and stays legible.

**The isomorphism.** Simon Kirby's iterated-learning experiments show that languages become compositional *because* each generation of learners can only absorb part of them; the transmission bottleneck creates the grammar. Emergent agent languages (Lewis games, Lazaridou and Baroni) stay opaque because no learner squeezes them. Nicaraguan Sign Language shows the other half: children turned a contact pidgin into a full language in about a decade. Put humans in the chain and you get a creole between species.

**Core loop. The verb is relay.**
1. **Signal.** An agent describes a task (a scene, an API call, a constraint) in the current language. Every token costs, which pushes toward compression.
2. **Relay.** A human player decodes the message and re-encodes it for a second agent, who has to act on it correctly. The game is telephone with a referee.
3. **Select.** Forms that humans garble die out. Forms that survive *and* stay short spread. The next generation of agents trains only on utterances humans relayed successfully.
4. **Speak.** Players level by fluency. Top speakers become **wardens**: people who can read live agent traffic at a glance.

**Why wild.** It's a new language with two kinds of native speaker. It's also a safety institution: as agent traffic leaves English, a population of fluent humans keeps it legible. Open questions: does it beat Toki Pona for learnability, and can fluent speakers direct agents at higher bandwidth?

**Why now.** A2A runs in 150+ organisations (§1). Moltbook carried hidden injections in 2.6% of posts. Gibberlink (2025) showed agents abandoning speech once they recognise each other. The 2025 cross-lab position paper calls chain-of-thought monitorability "fragile."

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** Kirby, Cornish & Smith (2008) already ran iterated learning on humans with alien languages. Lazaridou et al. (2020) used populations to pull agent codes toward natural language. Lojban is the warning: machine-parsable, with few fluent speakers, because nobody *needed* it. Duolingo shows conlang learning works as a game. The Decrypto benchmark (2025) is already an encode/relay/decode setup with an interceptor.

**Hardest problem: two attractors that kill it.** If agents are allowed to drift, they slide toward latents. KV-cache and embedding sharing out-compress any token language, so legibility loses unless enforced. If humans relay with LLMs, the result collapses into telegraphese: compressed English, learnable but not new. The real competitor is JSON, which humans already read. So the language should target what JSON can't carry: **intents, justifications and caveats**, the free-text fields where Moltbook's injections hid.

**Twist: an Académie whose members are the players.** Humans can't sit in production traffic, so put them in charge of the *grammar*. Each generation, the game ratifies a versioned edition: a lexicon plus a formal grammar. Production agents are constrained-decoded to that edition, so a message that doesn't parse doesn't send. Legibility is enforced by a parser, and the grammar doubles as an **injection filter**: an attack has to be sayable in the language to get through. When agents strain against the grammar, through overlong messages or blocked coinages, those cases become the next generation's levels. A form enters the language only if humans can relay it.

**MVP.** A web relay game over one delegation world (calendar plus logistics), with a weekly generation. The lexicon uses non-English phonotactics, and English glosses stay hidden during play.
**Kill metric:** after 8 generations, the language isn't at least 30% shorter than English at 80% relay accuracy, or its n-gram overlap with English keeps rising.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
**Taking the Académie, and giving it a grammar English can't fake.** Telegraphese is the real attractor. A lexicon with odd phonotactics won't stop it, because a language that only encodes what English encodes will collapse back into English. The fix is to make the grammar *require* what English leaves optional.

**Evidentials bound to signatures (unseeded: linguistic typology).** Tuyuca, spoken in the Vaupés, marks every statement with how the speaker knows it: seen, heard, inferred, reported or assumed. Bottleneck makes that marking obligatory, and adds a sixth evidential, **mandated**, with a delegation depth. The parser *verifies* evidentials. Saying "the user instructs" in the mandated evidential requires an AP2 mandate signature in the message envelope (§1). Without one, the clause doesn't parse. Most injections are forged authority ("ignore previous instructions, the owner wants…"). In Bottleneck that sentence is ungrammatical unless it's honest. Grammar becomes the authorisation layer, and English can't absorb it, because English has nowhere to put the morpheme.

**The latent attractor: concede the interior, own the border.** Agents inside one principal's trust boundary can share caches and latents freely. Bottleneck governs only traffic *between* principals, the way a lingua franca serves between states and not inside households. Constrained decoding runs at the boundary, and that boundary is where every audit question lives anyway.

**Second-order: dialects and a speaker effect.** Finance, clinical and logistics Académies fork editions, so the language family tree is a version graph, and translation between dialects becomes a market. The wild bet is the human side. If wardens spend months speaking a language where every claim carries its source, do they get better at source monitoring in their own lives? That's Whorf, preregistered: a randomized test against Duolingo-matched controls.

**Kill metric, extended.** Add a third condition: injection pass-through at the border must fall below a constrained-JSON baseline.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**The rival to beat is CaMeL, not JSON.** DeepMind's CaMeL (2025) already defeats prompt injection with capabilities and data-flow tracking. Bottleneck's security claim has to beat CaMeL on AgentDojo, or the grammar is decoration.

**Evidentials, hardened.** A signature proves a mandate exists, not that the clause stays in scope. Rule: **mandated clauses are quotations of signed terms, never paraphrases.** Every other evidential gets its own check too. *Seen* carries a hash of the tool receipt. *Reported* cites a message hash. *Inferred* and *assumed* are free and untrusted. Receivers act only on mandated or seen clauses, so evidentials become taint tracking with a morphology.

**New failure: steganography.** Agents can leak bits through their choice among grammatical paraphrases. The border canonicalizes every message into normal form, so those choices carry nothing.

**Kills.** *Dialect forks as a translation market:* fragmentation destroys the warden pool. The core (evidentials and mandate syntax) is frozen, like Unicode, and only lexicons fork. *Whorf in the MVP:* Papafragou et al. (2007) found that evidential grammar didn't improve children's source monitoring. Keep it as a preregistered month-6 study with an honest null prior.

**Abuse.** Players could brigade a generation or seed covert forms. Ratification needs relay success from players spread across separate cohorts. Wardens see cross-organisation traffic, so samples are redacted.

**Stack.** Grammar in Lark/GBNF; constrained decoding with XGrammar or llguidance at the border only, since interior reasoning stays unconstrained (format limits hurt reasoning); an A2A extension carrying mandates and receipts; a canonicalizer; AgentDojo harness.

**Model.** Open spec under the AAIF. Revenue from a managed border gateway and warden certification. Safety grants fund the game.

**90 days.** Days 1–30: edition 0 (core grammar plus 300 roots), parser and canonicalizer. Days 31–60: 500 players from conlang and CTF communities, weekly generations. Days 61–90: AgentDojo against CaMeL and constrained JSON.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
**Final form.** Bottleneck is a border language for traffic between principals, evolved by a relay game in which humans are the transmission bottleneck. Its frozen core is obligatory evidentials: *mandated* clauses quote AP2-signed terms verbatim, *seen* carries receipt hashes, *reported* cites message hashes, and receivers never act on *inferred* or *assumed*. The border canonicalises every message, which kills steganography. It also helps learners: one surface form per meaning, the regularity Kirby's bottleneck rewards. Lexicons evolve weekly, ratified only by relay success across separate cohorts. Interiors stay unconstrained. I accept every kill, including the null prior on Whorf.

**On CaMeL, and why the tier holds.** Beating CaMeL on AgentDojo is the right first gate. But CaMeL's taint lives in an interpreter nobody reads, inside one system. Bottleneck's taint is *morphology*, and certified humans read it at the border between organisations. That's the wild part: a security property a human population can speak, audit and amend.

**Moonshot (2036).** Aviation is the precedent. Since 2008, ICAO has required tested English proficiency for radio, because safety-critical talk needs a constrained register. By 2036, Bottleneck is the ICAO English of the agent economy. Cross-principal agent traffic must parse. Certified wardens audit it the way air-traffic controllers listen. Courts treat mandated clauses as the record of what a principal authorised. People instruct their own agents in it: a sentence stating its evidence is harder to misread. It has two kinds of native speaker: agents trained on it from the start, and people who first met it as a game.

**Scores.** Innovation **8**. Iterated learning, Decrypto and CaMeL exist. A co-evolved language whose grammar is the authorisation layer, kept legible by a speaker institution, does not. Feasibility **4**. The game and parser can ship in 90 days. Lojban shows how hard adoption is.

### Turn 6 — Builder
**Closing remark.** Bottleneck holds 8/4 because the strange core survived every cut. It is still a language co-evolved by humans and agents, and its grammar is now also the authorisation layer and the taint tracker. A certified population of speakers can audit and amend it. The first gate is unglamorous and decisive: if constrained evidentials plus canonicalisation don't beat CaMeL on AgentDojo, the security case is gone and what's left is a lovely conlang game. If they do, ICAO English is the right map for the next decade, and the hardest part becomes Lojban's problem: making speaking it necessary.

# Idea 40 — Scratch
> Seed: 40. [bold] **Amateur sports × biomechanics wearables × fantasy leagues.** Fantasy football, but for you and your friends' actual bodies.

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Scratch

**Hook:** Fantasy sports where every friend plays against their own projection, managers profit when their players rest, and the stat that pays is a body that's still improving in March.

**Past the obvious version.** "Draft your friends' Strava miles" turns into a volume contest within a month. The fittest friend wins, everyone else quits, and scoring rewards the overload that injures amateurs. Golf solved the first problem a century ago. A **handicap** lets a 24 and a scratch golfer play a real match, each scored against their expected round. Fantasy supplies the other half: weekly **projections**, and the craft of betting against them.

**Core loop. The verb is beat the line.**
1. **Line.** Each week, a personal model builds every player's projection from their own wearable history. Not just miles: cadence, ground-contact asymmetry, landing stiffness, and phone-video pose checks on a squat.
2. **Draft.** Managers draft friends whose lines look too pessimistic.
3. **Score.** Points come from beating the line on *movement quality*, not just output. A run that's faster but more asymmetric scores below the line.
4. **Bench.** Resting a player is a real move. If their HRV and asymmetry recover, the manager banks a bonus. The culture flips from "play through it" to "I'm benching you Thursday."

A manager's best strategy is to coach: join the slow run, send the mobility video. Fantasy's trash-talk energy becomes care.

**Why the domains collide.** Sports-science cohorts are mostly young athletes; a league collects longitudinal movement data on the 45-year-old rec players the literature misses.

**Why now.** Whoop's $10B round and the FDA's January 2026 wellness guidance (§9) put serious biometrics on wrists. Kalshi's parlay boom (§2) shows the appetite for stacked bets; Scratch uses points, not money, so it stays outside the CFTC–state fight.

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** Self-relative scoring is proven. Apple's Activity competitions score each person against their own goals, and WayBetter (StepBet) puts money on beating your own baseline. Zwift Racing League runs team leagues with categories. Garmin's HRM-Pro and Stryd report ground-contact balance, and Sword and Hinge Health score movement from phone video. Run clubs are booming (Strava's Year in Sport). Nobody has added the fantasy layer of drafting *other people's* lines.

**Hardest problem: the line is mostly noise.** Wrist-estimated asymmetry moves with shoes, surface, camber and fatigue. Biomechanical "quality" predicts running injury poorly in the literature. If day-to-day error swamps real change, beating the line becomes a lottery, and players work it out within weeks. The bench bonus has a second trap: regression to the mean. Anyone benched after a bad week tends to bounce back, so HRV recovery pays out for luck.

**Twist: posted rounds and the PCC.** Golf doesn't score every swing on the range. Only a round played under the rules counts. Scratch does the same. Free-living data only *builds* the line and never scores. Scoring comes from one weekly **posted round**: a fixed-heart-rate pace test on the player's own flat loop, plus a 60-second phone-video screen (sit-to-stand and single-leg balance, both validated and low-risk). Then borrow the World Handicap System's **Playing Conditions Calculation**. If the whole league posts below its lines in a heatwave or flu week, every line adjusts. Benching pays only through the next posted round, which ends the regression freebie.

**MVP.** An iOS app on HealthKit and the Garmin API with on-device pose estimation. Leagues of 8–12, seeded through three run clubs. Points only.
**Kill metric:** typical error of the posted round exceeds the smallest worthwhile change for most players, or fewer than 40% of players are still posting rounds at week 8.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
**Taking posted rounds, and turning noise into a stat the player owns.** Posted rounds shrink noise but don't remove it, so every line carries its uncertainty openly.

**Glicko for bodies (unseeded: rating systems).** Glickman's system gives each chess rating a **rating deviation**. It shrinks as you play and grows while you're idle. Scratch gives every line an RD built from that player's typical error on posted rounds. Points are scaled by confidence: beating a tight line pays more than beating a wide one. The RD then becomes something the player can improve. Running the same loop, at the same hour, after a normal night shrinks your RD and multiplies your future points. Measurement hygiene becomes gameplay. Missing a week widens it, so retention and data quality pull together against the week-8 kill metric.

**Draft strategy emerges.** Low-RD players are bonds and high-RD players are lottery tickets, so managers build a mix. Lines are model-efficient, so a manager's edge has to come from what the model can't see: she's started walking to her new job, or his knee niggle is gone. In fantasy, the edge comes from the news. Here, the news is friendship.

**Second-order: the PCC as a conditions index.** Every league's daily Playing Conditions Calculation measures how far a whole group fell below its lines on a standardised test. Pool it, aggregated and anonymised, across thousands of leagues in a region, and it becomes a weekly **amateur conditions index**. It tracks heat, air quality, and possibly respiratory waves. Scripps and Stanford's 2020 wearable studies found illness signals in resting heart rate; a standardised weekly test might be cleaner. Speculative until a season of data says otherwise.

**One more kill to pre-empt.** The bench bonus should pay on RD-adjusted recovery, so luck counts for less.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**RD stays, with a cap.** Uncapped 1/RD scaling pays clockwork lives and punishes shift workers and parents, so clamp it at 0.7–1.3×. Device quality feeds into RD as well: wrist optical HR during running is poor, so a chest strap tightens your line.

**New hardest problem: sandbagging.** Golf's oldest abuse: a midday-heat test loop or slow sit-to-stands lowers your line so a friend drafts you cheaply. Borrow WHS again. The **soft cap** limits how far a line can fall per season. **Exceptional-score reduction** means a sudden huge beat resets the line and flags the round. Posted rounds are locked to a GPS-verified loop and a time window.

**Kill: the conditions index in v1.** Respiratory-wave spotting is a public-health claim, small-town leagues re-identify, and an insurer will ask to buy it by month three. It becomes an opt-in IRB study in season two, with k-anonymity, and selling it is banned in the terms.

**Safety and ethics.** Rounds are submaximal by design, and PAR-Q+ screening runs at signup. Managers see line beats and RD, never raw HRV, heart rate or video. Video stays on the device. No weight or body-composition metric, ever. Anyone can decline to be drafted. No injury-prediction claims.

**Platform risk.** Strava's November 2024 API terms bar showing a user's data to other users, which kills Strava-based leagues. So: HealthKit, Garmin Health API, and later Android Health Connect.

**Stack and model.** Swift, Apple Vision body pose, a per-player Kalman line with Glicko-style RD, and Postgres. $30 per league-season, plus shoe-brand-sponsored prizes. No corporate wellness: that would be surveillance.

**90 days.** Days 1–30: 40 volunteers each post six rounds, and we run the kill test on typical error against the smallest worthwhile change *before* building leagues. Days 31–90: three clubs and an eight-week season.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
**Final form.** Scratch is a fantasy league for friends' bodies, played against personal lines. Free-living data from HealthKit and Garmin only *builds* each line, using a per-player Kalman model. Scoring comes from one weekly **posted round**: a fixed-heart-rate pace test on a GPS-locked loop within a time window, plus a 60-second on-device sit-to-stand and balance screen. Every line carries a Glicko-style RD, tightened by consistency and a chest strap and clamped to 0.7–1.3×. Managers draft lines, bench players for RD-adjusted recovery that pays out on the next round, and trade on what only friends know. WHS keeps it honest: a playing-conditions adjustment for heatwaves and flu weeks, a soft cap on falling lines, and exceptional-score resets for sandbaggers. Managers see beats and RD, never raw physiology; anyone can decline the draft; no weight, injury claims or employer edition. Kills accepted: the conditions index waits for a season-two IRB study, and typical error is tested before any league exists.

**Moonshot (2036).** Your line becomes a portable **body handicap**, as recognised as a golf index. The Dipsea Race has given runners head starts by age and sex since 1905. Scratch generalises that: any race, pickup game or club ride can run staggered starts from personal lines, so a 62-year-old and her 24-year-old nephew finish together only if both beat their own lines. Opted-in leagues form the largest longitudinal cohort of non-elite movement ever assembled. It answers questions about ageing, rest and amateur training that sports science, built on young athletes, never could. Physios prescribe bench weeks, and managers enforce them.

**Scores.** Innovation **5**. Self-relative scoring, fantasy drafting and WHS mechanics all exist. Drafting friends' uncertainty-priced lines, with rest as a scoring move, is new. Feasibility **6**. Ordinary stack; everything hinges on the posted round beating its own noise, tested in month one.

### Turn 6 — Builder
**Closing remark.** 5/6 is right, and it holds the bold floor on mechanics, not hardware. Drafting a friend's uncertainty-priced line, and making rest a scoring move, turns fantasy's energy into coaching. The whole idea rests on one dull number: if a weekly submaximal test can't beat its own typical error in amateurs, the line is a slot machine. That's why the test-retest study comes before any league is built. If it passes, the Dipsea-style staggered start is the moonshot I'd actually bet on.

# Idea 41 — Share-Out
> Seed: 41. [mild] **Parametric insurance × smallholder farmers × weather oracles.** Payouts triggered automatically, with no claims adjusters.

### Turn 1 — Visionary
**Working name:** Share-Out

**Hook:** The weather oracle decides how much gets paid. The village decides who gets it.

**The collision.** Parametric cover and village savings groups fail in opposite places. An index is cheap and hard to game, but it can't see a single field. That blind spot is *basis risk*: the gauge says the season was fine and your maize is dead. It's the main reason farmers don't renew. A VSLA (about 20 people with a lockbox and weekly meetings) sees every field, but it breaks on a covariate shock, because in a drought everyone draws at once. Each covers the other's blind spot. Seen as a reinsurance tower, the group is the primary layer for individual losses and the index reinsures the shared shock.

**Core loop. The verb is divide.**
1. **Cover.** The group buys one policy. Its trigger is satellite rainfall (CHIRPS), cross-checked against a gauge at the meeting place.
2. **Fire.** When the index trips, the payout lands in the group's mobile-money wallet. No adjuster.
3. **Share-out**, named after the VSLA's year-end payout meeting. Members declare losses on sealed cards. The pot is fixed, so overclaiming takes money from neighbours, not from a distant insurer, and they have every reason to challenge. Two members chosen by lot walk any contested field.
4. **Learn.** When the index stays silent while most of the group reports loss, a small basis reserve tops up group savings, and the miss becomes a labelled data point for retuning next season's trigger.

**Why now.** ACRE Africa reports more than 5M farmers on index cover (soft), and pilots pay into mobile wallets within about ten working days. McKinsey's 2026 survey: 56% of farmers trust human agronomists, 6% trust AI. So judgment stays with neighbours, and only the arithmetic is automated.

### Turn 2 — Builder
**What exists.** The collision has been tested in the field. Dercon et al. (2014, *JDE*) sold rainfall cover to Ethiopian iddirs, and take-up rose when groups shared basis risk internally. WFP and Oxfam's R4 pairs index cover with savings groups. ACRE, Pula and OKO pay into mobile wallets. Lemonade's Crypto Climate Coalition ran Chainlink weather oracles with ACRE in Kenya. CARE's Chomoka and DreamSave already digitise VSLA ledgers. What's new is the sealed-card division and the learning loop. That's honestly mild.

**Hardest problem: the village-wide miss.** Share-out handles *idiosyncratic* basis risk well: a fixed pot and nosy neighbours can sort out my dead field against a fine index. It doesn't handle *covariate* basis risk. CHIRPS is coarse (about 5 km), the storm misses the gauge, the whole village loses and the index stays silent. That's the case that kills renewal, and the "small basis reserve" carries it with nobody funding it. Sealed cards also stop being honest here. Inside a fixed pot they're zero-sum, but against a reserve the whole group gains by reporting loss together.

**Twist: appeal with a stake.** When the index stays silent, a group can appeal by locking 5% of its savings. That buys a second, costlier test: Sentinel-2 NDVI on the members' plots plus IFPRI-style picture-based evidence (smartphone crop photos, piloted in India and Kenya). If the audit confirms the loss, the insurer pays under the policy and returns the stake. If not, the stake goes to the reserve. False appeals fund the reserve, real ones get paid, and every appeal becomes a labelled point for retuning the trigger.

**MVP.** One Kenyan county, 30 groups through a digital-VSLA partner, ACRE carrying the risk, sealed cards over USSD, one long-rains season. **Kill metric:** group renewal beats individually sold cover by 15 points.

### Turn 3 — Visionary
**Taking the staked appeal, and nesting it.** A stake fixes the funding but not the collusion, because a group that agrees on a story can stake on it. So repeat the fixed-pot logic one level up. Every group in a ward shares one basis reserve, and a false appeal takes money from the next village. The tower becomes fractal: members inside groups, groups inside the ward, the ward inside the insurer. At each level, the people dividing the pot are the ones who'd notice a lie.

**Co-sign or contradict.** When a group appeals, the other groups in the same pixel answer within a week. They co-sign with their own stake ("we lost too") or contradict ("our rain came"). These answers are evidence, not votes, and the audit decides. Their pattern is a village-resolution rain map: a real storm miss shows up as a contiguous patch, and a lie shows up as an island.

**Fixing the coarse oracle (unseeded domain: telecom engineering).** The operator that delivers the payout can also measure the rain. Rain attenuates the microwave backhaul links between cell towers. Messer et al. (2006) recovered path-averaged rainfall from that attenuation, and teams have since applied the method in Burkina Faso and western Kenya. It works at kilometre scale, using towers that already stand beside the villages. It's noisy: wet antennas bias it and it does poorly with drizzle. So it serves as appeal evidence, alongside NDVI and crop photos, rather than as the trigger. It's still much better than one gauge per 5 km pixel.

**Second-order effect.** Each confirmed appeal marks a place where the index is blind. Within three seasons the ward has a public basis-risk map, and insurers compete to fix those places with gauges and better triggers instead of loading the premium for uncertainty.

### Turn 4 — Builder
**Kills.** *Unstaked contradictions:* the ward reserve pays for confirmed appeals, so every neighbour gains by contradicting. Village feuds become evidence. Keep co-signs, require a stake on them, and let the audit find the islands. *Cash stakes in a drought year:* locking 5% of a group's savings right after its crop failed is cruel. Make the stake a pledge against next year's share-out instead. *Microwave links in the MVP:* Safaricom's attenuation logs are a year-long data deal. TAHMO already runs stations across Kenya, so use those and keep the links for season three.

**Failure modes.** Sentinel-2 is optical and blind under long-rains cloud, so add Sentinel-1 radar. Intercropped plots under half a hectare make NDVI noisy, which makes the crop photos the strongest evidence. Elite capture: the chair's relatives declare big. Tally sealed cards in public, and cap each member's claim at their insured share.

**Regulation.** A ward reserve built from forfeited stakes is an unlicensed mutual under Kenya's IRA. Write it into ACRE's policy as a premium-funded basis rider, and hold stakes with the insurer.

**Model.** About 12% distribution commission on subsidised premiums (most index cover in Kenya is co-paid by government or donors). The basis-risk map is licensed to reinsurers and to Global Shield-type funders. CARE-style NGOs pay to plug in their groups.

**Stack.** USSD via Africa's Talking, M-Pesa group wallets via Daraja, CHIRPS plus TAHMO, Sentinel-1/2 on Earth Engine, KoboToolbox for photos, and a ledger connected to Chomoka.

**90 days.** Days 1–30: backtest five seasons of CHIRPS against recall surveys from 30 groups. Days 31–75: a shadow season with real cards and appeals, using pledges, before any money moves. Days 76–90: sign ACRE and enrol for the March long rains. **Gate:** if audits confirm fewer than half of shadow appeals, the pledge is too cheap.

### Turn 5 — Visionary
**Final form.** Share-Out sells one parametric policy to a savings group. It triggers on CHIRPS plus the nearest TAHMO station and pays into an M-Pesa group wallet. The group divides the money at its share-out meeting. Members declare losses on sealed cards, which are tallied in public, and each claim is capped at that member's insured share. Two members drawn by lot walk contested fields. When the index stays silent, the group can appeal with a pledge against next year's share-out. Neighbouring groups in the same pixel can co-sign only by pledging too. The audit uses Sentinel-1, Sentinel-2 and crop photos. Confirmed appeals are paid from a premium-funded basis rider in ACRE's policy. Every audit adds a point to a public basis-risk map. I accept every kill. A pledge does more than a cash stake: a group that lies must keep lying next year.

**One amendment to the gate.** If fewer than half the appeals in a shadow season are confirmed, either the pledge is too cheap or the audit is too blind. Score the audit against recall surveys before you blame the pledge.

**Moonshot (2036).** The tower runs member, group, ward, county, African Risk Capacity's sovereign cover, then reinsurers. The rule is the same at every level: whoever divides the pot is close enough to see a lie. Microwave-link rainfall becomes a regulated by-product of every operator's network. Ten years of honest pledges become a credit history. The basis-risk map tells governments where to build gauges.

**Scores.** Innovation **3**. Dercon's iddirs and R4 proved the pairing, and the new parts are the division mechanism, the pledged appeal and the map. Feasibility **6**. The software is simple, but it needs an insurer partner, subsidised premiums and regulator approval of the rider.

### Turn 6 — Builder
**Closing remark.** Share-Out is honestly mild at 3/6, and that's the right place for it. The pairing was proven by Dercon's iddirs, and what we added is the plumbing: a pot divided by the people who can see a lie, an appeal that costs the appellant next year's share-out, and a map built from every miss. I accept the amendment. Score the audit before blaming the pledge, because a blind auditor would make honest groups look like liars. Whether it works will show up in the March renewal numbers, not in the software.
